기록상 결론은 **범위 밖**입니다.

- `mcp-server`에 포함된 것은 MCP 도구 등록·I/O 계약, Vault 파서/라이터, 온톨로지 컴파일·그래프 질의, 안전장치입니다.
- 반대로 **`AST/source search engine`은 명시적으로 제외**되어 있습니다. [mcp-server.md](/Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/docs/ontology/capabilities/mcp-server.md:93)
- 프로젝트 방향도 심볼 위치·호출자 탐색은 grep, 언어 서버, AST 인덱스, CodeGraph의 역할이며 Atlas는 코드 인덱스를 대체하지 않는다고 기록합니다. [README.md](/Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/README.md:479)
- 유효한 결정 원장에는 필요해질 경우 **별도의 `symbol/CodeGraph integration boundary`를 설계한다**고 되어 있습니다. 현재 `mcp-server` 경계에 포함시킨다는 결정은 없습니다. [DECISIONS.md](/Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/docs/DECISIONS.md:7064)

따라서 기록된 구분은 이렇습니다.

- 심볼 검색 도구를 MCP에 등록하고 I/O 계약으로 노출하는 부분: `mcp-server` 범위
- 심볼을 인덱싱하고 정의·호출 관계를 검색하는 기능 자체: `mcp-server` capability 범위 밖
- 별도 capability나 통합 경계로 둘지: 아직 결정되지 않았고, 별도 경계를 설계할 가능성만 기록됨

vs 상호작용석 「지금 배선하면 안 된다」.", "+   **적용 규칙: 최소 슬라이스 → 상호작용석 채택.** 상호작용석 원문: *\"신뢰도가 확정되기 전에 없는 신호를 있는 척 배선하면 「명시적 거짓」(#828 이 이미 잡은 부류)을 새로 만드는 꼴이다. 지금 할 수 있는 최소 조치는 하드코딩을 없애 넷을 동등하게 만드는 것뿐.\"* 작업대석 자신도 같은 위험을 반대 논점으로 적었다 — 파일 존재는 「지금 쓰는 도구」가 아니라 **「예전에 한 번 쓴 도구」**를 가리킬 수 있다.", "+", "+3. **카드 크롬을 이번에 걷을 것인가.** 위계석 「미루자」 vs 체계석 「동반 필수」 — **충돌이 아니었다.** 재보니 서로 다른 크기를 말하고 있었다: 위계석이 미룬 것은 **카피 통합**(두 표면 × 입력 칸은 최고빈도 표면이고 내 헌장이 그 예산을 `0~--motion-fast` 로 못박는다. `--motion-base`(180ms)는 내가 내 규칙을 어긴 것이다.\"*", "+**반증 조건**: 자람을 켠 뒤 **「타자가 무겁다」는 보고**가 나오면 반대가 옳았다. 그때 답은 임의로 깎는 것이 아니라 `--motion-fast`(120ms)로 **한 "+**반증 조건**: 채택됐으므로 이 반대는 이겼다. 반대 방향의 반증 — 포털 전환 후 다른 표면에서 팝오버 위치 회귀가 관측되면 프리미티브 전환이 성급했던 것이다.", "+", "+**3. 작업대석 — 웹 CORS 관측의 수명.**", "+> *\"「웹에서 Ollama 가 CORS 를 막지 않는다」는 이 기계의 이 순간의 기본값일 뿐이다. Ollama 가 `OLLAMA_ORIGINS` 기본값을 강화하면(보안 커뮤니티가 이미 요구해 온 방향) 이 관측은 뒤집힌다.\"*", "+**반증 조건**: Ollama 릴리스가 기본 오리진 정책을 좁히면 관측이 뒤집힌다. **그래서 문구를 「CORS 때문에 안 된다」가 아니라 「이 러너의 CORS 설정과 무관하게, 이 앱이 웹에는 전송 경로를 두지 않기로 선택했다」로 썼다** — 기본값이 바뀌어도 참인 문장이다. 웹 게이트를 여는 조건은 **po-steward 판정 대기**(질문 3, 미도착 — 도착 시 후속 기록).", "+", "+**4. 지킴이 — 코드 존재가 동작 증명은 아니다.**", "+> *\"쓰기 파이프라인이 코드상 있다고 해서 사용자 경험상 작동하는 건 아니다. 코드 존재만으로 「신설  **누구의 버튼인지**를 더 이상 말하지 않는다. 막대는 탐색을 없앤다: 매번", "+     같은 자리에 있으면 눈이 찾지 않는다.", "+3. **나머지 넷의 기본값은 오늘 그대로다** — 확장 구조 `disc`(나선 원반) ·", "+   한 번에 여는 개수 24 · 이름을 시도할 개수 8 · 동시에 펼쳐 둘 부모 3.", "+   세 숫자는 **새 docs/benchmark/results/2026-08-25-codex-B1-on.txt:1841:./src/entities/docs-vault/data/content.json:2:  "AGENT-GRAPH-WORKFLOW": "# Agent Graph Workflow\n\n> Current as of 2026-08-02. This is the user-facing guide for running\n> `ontology-atlas` as a local meaning graph: CLI-only, MCP-connected, and web\n> workbench flows over the same markdown vault.\n\n`ontology-atlas` is not a hosted graph database. It is a local-first graph\nworkbench where markdown frontmatter is the graph, git is the audit log, and AI\nagents can read or write through MCP when they are connected.\n\n> **How the server reaches you (decided 2026-07-27, `docs/DECISIONS.md`):**\n> npm publishing is retired. There is no `ontology-atlas` or\n> `ontology-atlas-mcp` package to install, and there never will be. Two live\n> paths instead:\n>\n> - **Installed app** — open the vault folder and press **Connect agent**. The app carries a compiled MCP server in its own bundle,\n>   shows you the config it is about to write, writes it, then spawns the\n>   server and round-trips `get_concept` before reporting success.\n> - **Source checkout** — register the server as\n>   `node /absolute/path/to/ontology-atlas/mcp/src/index.js` and run CLI proofs\n>   as `node cli/src/index.mjs <command>`.\n>\n> A config still holding `command: \"npx\"` predates the bundled server and\n> cannot start.\n>\n> Command examples below are written as `ontology-atlas <command>` for\n> readability — that is the CLI's name, not a global binary. From a source\n> checkout, run each as `node cli/src/index.mjs <command>`.\n\n## Official Client Contract\n\nChecked against official docs on 2026-06-04:\n\n- Codex supports MCP servers in the CLI and IDE extension, stores MCP server\n  configuration in Codex `config.toml`, and exposes `codex mcp list` plus the\n  `/mcp` TUI panel for checking active servers.\n  Project-scoped `.codex/config.toml` is ignored until Codex marks that project\n  trusted; `codex mcp list` from the trusted folder and `connection_info` are\n  therefore required connection evidence, not optional diagnostics.\n  Source: https://developers.openai.com/codex/mcp\n- Claude Code configures MCP servers with `claude mcp`, checks connected\n  servers with `claude mcp list` and `/mcp`, and supports local stdio servers\n  for tools that need direct system access.\n  Source: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp\n\nAtlas keeps the Codex boundary machine-readable too: `agent-setup --json`\nreports `clientStatus.codex.projectTrust: \"unknown\"` and\n`registration: \"unverified\"` until a fresh Codex session passes the trust\nprompt, `codex mcp list`, and Atlas `connection_info`. If the project config is\nignored, use the generated global `codex mcp add ...` fallback, restart Codex,\nand repeat those checks. Atlas cannot inspect Codex credentials or trust\ndirectly, so config readiness never becomes a live-connection claim.\n\nOntology Atlas therefore does not reimplement Claude Code, Codex, or Cursor chat\ninside the app — it does not own an agent loop, model routing, API keys, or\nbilling. It prepares the local MCP files, root-specific commands, restart\nguidance, and verification gates so those agents can connect from their own app,\nterminal, or IDE session and work against the same vault.\n\n**Reversal record, 2026-07-26 — the embedded terminal is gone.** Earlier that\nsame day this section said the desktop app *may host a terminal*, and a bottom\ndock shipped that ran your already-installed `claude` / `codex` CLI. The owner\nreversed it within the week, and the reasoning is kept here rather than deleted.\n\nWhy it was removed: the dock's own trust contract made it a strict subset of the\nterminal you already use — one login shell with no arguments, no tabs, no\nsplits, no shell profiles, the session ending when you collapsed it, and about\nthirteen visible rows on a 14-inch screen. Every one of those was the right\ncall, and together they meant long agent loops, resumed sessions, and parallel\nworktrees belonged somewhere else. The synergy that seemed to justify embedding\n— *the agent edits the vault and the map reacts immediately* — turned out to be\nposition-independent: the vault watcher observes the folder on disk, so an agent\nrunning in iTerm moves the map exactly the same way. Polishing the window did\nnot change the comparison, and sunk cost is not a reason to keep a surface.\n\n**Atlas does not host a terminal; it hands off to yours.** The bridge between a\nperson and an agent is a protocol, not a window: the MCP tools, the CLI, the\n`agent-brief` handoff prompt, and the vault watcher. The vault agent panel gives\nyou a copy block — `cd <your vault>` plus the sentence to paste — and you run it\nwhere your session already lives. What you change out there comes back through\nthe watcher and the recent-change lens on the map.\n\n**Nothing runs on its own.** Atlas never issues a command, spawns a process, or\nstarts an agent on your behalf. It prepares files and text you choose to run.\n\n## Choose The Right Mode\n\nUse this table before setup. The modes share the same vault files, so switching\nbetween them does not migrate data.\n\n| Situation | Start here | What you get |\n|---|---|---|\n| You only want to inspect a local vault from Terminal | CLI-only | Validation, workspace summaries, graph scans, path/explain queries, and graph DB packs without any MCP client |\n| Claude Code, Codex, or Cursor is connected to MCP | MCP-connected | The agent can call read/write tools directly, receive structured repair fields, and update the markdown vault after validation |\n| You want graph-database-style exploration but not a database server | Graph DB pack | Bounded query plans, node/edge scans, domain matrix, paths, relation explanations, and follow-up evidence commands |\n| Setup is unclear or you opened the agent from another codebase root | Agent setup gate | Config repair commands, restart guidance, JSON readiness checks, and fallback timing before edits |\n\nThe installed app *does* claim a one-click agent connection, because it carries\nthe server it is connecting you to. Press **Connect agent**: the\napp shows the config it is about to write, writes it after you approve, then\nspawns the bundled binary and round-trips `get_concept` — a green light means\nyour vault is readable, not that a process started. Source-checkout contributors\nregister the local MCP entry point instead, restart their agent, run the JSON\ngate, and only then ask the agent to write ontology updates. A browser surface\nhas no absolute path to write, so it stays honestly demoted to the\nsource-checkout instructions rather than emitting a config that cannot boot.\n\nRead the JSON gate in three states:\n\n- `ok: false` means setup or fallback command execution is broken. Fix the\n  config before asking the agent to edit ontology files.\n- `ok: true` with `performanceOk: false` means the local graph path works, but\n  fallback latency is slow enough to inspect before relying on it heavily.\n- `ok: true` with `performanceOk: true` means the setup and fallback graph path\n  are ready for read-first agent work.\n\nFor humans, `agent-brief --verify-fallbacks` prints the same setup gate summary\nbefore the row list: `ok=true performanceOk=true wall=... slow=0/N failed=0`.\nThat line is the fastest way to tell whether a connector-less Claude Code/Codex\nsession can trust the local CLI graph path before scanning or writing.\n\n## What Works Without MCP Connected\n\nYou can use the product without connecting Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, or any\nMCP client.\n\nThe CLI currently exposes 54 commands over the same local vault, including\ngraph-database-style queries:\n\n```bash\nnode $ATLAS/cli/src/index.mjs validate docs/ontology\nnode $ATLAS/cli/src/index.mjs workspace-brief docs/ontology\nnode $ATLAS/cli/src/index.mjs match-nodes docs/ontology --kind capability --limit 10\nnode $ATLAS/cli/src/index.mjs match-edges docs/ontology --type depends_on --limit 10\nnode $ATLAS/cli/src/index.mjs domain-matrix docs/ontology --types depends_on,relates\nnode $ATLAS/cli/src/index.mjs agent-brief docs/ontology --graph-db-pack\n```\n\nThis mode is useful when an agent has no MCP connector available. The CLI still\nuses the same local graph engine and the same vault files; it just prints the\nanswers in terminal form instead of returning JSON-RPC tool results to an agent.\n\nUse `agent-setup` when the vault already exists and you only want to repair\nagent config files:\n\n```bash\nnode $ATLAS/cli/src/index.mjs agent-setup /absolute/path/to/vault --root /absolute/path/to/codebase --write\n```\n\nThat command creates missing `.mcp.json` and `.codex/config.toml` files without\nadding starter markdown. For a parseable existing file, it atomically merges or\nrebinds only the `ontology-atlas` JSON entry / TOML section pair and preserves\nunrelated servers, sections, and comments. Invalid or duplicate Atlas config is\nleft untouched with a merge template and a nonzero review result.\nIts terminal and JSON output also point back to this guide\n(`docs/AGENT-GRAPH-WORKFLOW.md`), so CLI-only setup logs still tell a human\nwhere to read the MCP, graph DB, and verification differences.\nThe same guide path is included in `agent-brief --prompt` and\n`agent-brief --graph-db-pack`, so the agent handoff and connector-less graph\nquery script carry the explanation forward after setup. The normal\n`agent-brief` terminal view and the shell-pasteable `--graph-db-pack` header now\nalso render the same mode guide directly, so a human can tell when to stay\nCLI-only, when MCP adds value, when to use the graph DB pack, and when to run the\nsetup gate without opening JSON first.\nThe `/ontology/insights` graph DB query pack card shows the same mode guide\nbefore its copy buttons, and the copied UI CLI pack includes the guide too, so\nthe explanation survives when a non-developer passes only the runbook into a\nfresh Claude Code or Codex session.\n`agent-setup --json` also includes `docs.modeComparison`, a machine-readable\nversion of the CLI-only / MCP-connected / graph DB pack / setup gate choice, so\nan AI agent can explain the right path without scraping this Markdown table.\nFor automation, `agent-brief --json` and MCP `query_ontology({operation:\n\"agent_brief\"})` expose the same location as `docs.workflowGuide`, so an AI\ntool does not need to parse the human prompt to find the guide.\nThey also expose this page's mode chooser as `docs.modeComparison` and the\nscan-to-proof rules as `docs.graphScanProofChecklist`, so an AI tool can inspect\nthe CLI-only / MCP-connected / graph DB pack / setup gate choice and the\nrequired `totalMatches` / follow-up / `evidence.pathsComplete` steps without\nparsing Markdown.\n\n## What MCP Adds\n\nMCP is the agent interface. When Claude Code, Codex, or Cursor has the\n`ontology-atlas` MCP server registered, `tools/list` provides the exact current\nlocal read/write inventory. Read tools cover connection and git state, node and\nevidence lookup, graph queries, validation, compilation, and source analysis.\nWrite tools cover guarded concept/relation changes, source binding, local\nsnapshots, and project-meaning finalization. Run `mcp-verify` to prove that the\nadvertised set, initialize guidance, and this vault agree.\n\nMCP adds three things that terminal-only use does not provide as naturally:\n\n1. The agent can fetch precise context on demand instead of asking the user to\n   paste terminal output.\n2. The agent can write back to the same markdown vault after it has read,\n   validated, and preflighted the change.\n3. Tool responses include structured repair fields, result contracts, and\n   write guardrails so the agent can recover from bad inputs without guessing.\n\nThe first MCP calls should be read-only:\n\n```json\n{ \"tool\": \"validate_vault\", \"arguments\": {} }\n{ \"tool\": \"query_ontology\", \"arguments\": { \"operation\": \"workspace_brief\" } }\n{ \"tool\": \"query_ontology\", \"arguments\": { \"operation\": \"agent_brief\" } }\n{ \"tool\": \"query_ontology\", \"arguments\": { \"operation\": \"health\" } }\n```\n\nOnly after those checks are clean should an agent propose writes.\n\nAfter the person accepts project competency answers and the agent has completed\nthe related concept/relation writes, `validate_vault`, and a complete compile,\ncall `finalize_project_meaning({ projectSlug, expected_mtime })`. The server\nderives the body/graph/source provenance and writes only the receipt—never raw\nanswers, witness text, a private absolute source root, or remote coordinates.\n`ok: true` means that receipt write completed; it does not mean the project is\nverified. Read `agent_brief.meaningAssessment` for the categorical fail-closed\nstate (`verified_current`, `review_required`, `needs_evidence`, or `invalid`).\nFor a multi-project vault, use `query_ontology({ operation: \"agent_brief\",\nproject: \"SLUG\" })` or `ontology-atlas agent-brief <vault> --project SLUG`.\n\n## How This Differs From A Graph Database\n\n`ontology-atlas` deliberately borrows graph DB query habits, but optimizes for a\ndifferent job.\n\n| Need | Graph DB | ontology-atlas |\n|---|---|---|\n| Storage | Server/database files | Plain markdown files in your repo or local folder |\n| Setup | Database service, schema, credentials | Pick or create a folder; no login or backend |\n| Query language | Cypher/Gremlin/SPARQL style | CLI commands and MCP `query_ontology` operations |\n| Source of truth | Database state | Git-tracked markdown frontmatter |\n| Human readability | Usually requires UI/export | Every node is an editable `.md` document |\n| Agent use | Agent needs DB tooling and schema context | Agent gets MCP tools, first-call guidance, result contracts, and write guardrails |\n| Write safety | Transaction/schema constraints | dry-runs, `relation_check`, `expected_mtime`, validation, maintenance queues |\n| Best scale | Large transactional graphs | Codebase/team memory graphs that need explainable local context |\n\nSo the claim is not \"faster than every graph DB at every graph workload.\" The\nclaim is narrower and more useful for this product: for a developer's local\ncodebase memory, it is more practical because the graph is inspectable, editable,\ngit-reviewable, and directly available to AI coding agents.\n\n## Graph-DB-Style Query Pack\n\nFor agent or terminal sessions, start with a plan-first scan instead of pulling\nthe full graph:\n\n```bash\nnode $ATLAS/cli/src/index.mjs facets docs/ontology --limit 10\nnode $ATLAS/cli/src/index.mjs schema docs/ontology --limit 10\nnode $ATLAS/cli/src/index.mjs match-nodes docs/ontology --kind capability --limit 10\nnode $ATLAS/cli/src/index.mjs match-edges docs/ontology --type depends_on --limit 10\nnode $ATLAS/cli/src/index.mjs domain-matrix docs/ontology --types depends_on,relates\nnode $ATLAS/cli/src/index.mjs all-paths capabilities/cli-developer-entry capabilities/mcp-server docs/ontology --plan --force --max-hops 3 --types depends_on,relates\nnode $ATLAS/cli/src/index.mjs explain capabilities/cli-developer-entry capabilities/mcp-server docs/ontology --types depends_on,relates\n```\n\nImportant rule: scan rows are candidates, not proof. Before using a node or edge\nfor an onboarding, refactor, or write decision, follow up with `node`,\n`match-edges`, `blast-radius`, `explain`, or `relation-check`.\n\nUse this scan-to-proof checklist:\n\n1. Report `totalMatches`, `limited`, and returned row count from `match-nodes`\n   or `match-edges`.\n2. For a node row, run `node` / `node_profile` or `blast-radius` before using it\n   as onboarding or refactor evidence.\n3. For an edge row, run `explain`, `path`, and `relation-check` before using it\n   as coupling or write evidence.\n4. For path evidence, report `evidence.pathsComplete`; if it is false, narrow\n   the query before writing or making an architecture claim.\n\n## Actual Verification Snapshot\n\nThese checks were run against this repository's dogfood vault on 2026-07-27.\n\nCLI-only checks:\n\n- `node cli/src/index.mjs agent-setup docs/ontology --json`\n  - `operation: \"agent_setup\"`\n  - `sideEffect: false`\n  - `summary: { total: 4, ready: 2, missing: 2, review: 0, written: 0, examples: 0 }`\n  - `modeIds: [\"cli_only\", \"mcp_connected\", \"graph_db_pack\", \"setup_gate\"]`\n- `node cli/src/index.mjs match-nodes docs/ontology --kind capability --min-degree 2 --sort degree --limit 8 --json`\n  - `operation: \"match_nodes\"`\n  - `totalMatches: 38`\n  - `returned: 8`\n  - `limited: true`\n  - `followUp.focusSlug: \"capabilities/cli-developer-entry\"`\n- `node cli/src/index.mjs agent-brief docs/ontology --verify-fallbacks --json --fallback-timeout-ms 15000 --fallback-slow-ms 5000 --fallback-concurrency 4`\n  - `operation: \"agent_fallback_check\"`\n  - `ok: true`\n  - `performanceOk: true`\n  - `total: 32`\n  - `passed: 32`\n  - `failed: 0`\n  - `slow: 0`\n  - `wallMs: 1993`\n  - `totalMs: 7928`\n  - slowest fallback: `match-edges --plan --types depends_on --limit 20`\n    at `377ms`\n- `node scripts/perf-graph.mjs --json --check --n=1000`\n  - budgets: `compileMs <= 750`, `queryMs <= 750`\n  - failures: `0`\n  - 1000 generated nodes, 3867 generated edges\n  - median `compile.fullMs: 18.00`\n  - median `agent_brief: 25.26ms`\n  - median `graph_db_pack: 24.55ms`\n  - median `project_map: 8.16ms`\n  - graph DB pack replayed 10 calls:\n    `query_plan`, `match_nodes`, `query_plan`, `match_edges`,\n    `domain_matrix`, `query_plan`, `centrality`, `query_plan`, `all_paths`,\n    `explain_relation`\n  - graph DB pack diagnostics: `totalMatches: [719, 718]`,\n    `allPathsEvidenceStatus: \"complete\"`, and\n    `explainRelationHasShortestPath: true`\n\nGraph and MCP-connected facts. **This section states no counts, hashes, or file\ntotals** — every one of them changes the moment anyone adds a node, so a number\nwritten here is wrong by the next commit and nobody notices. Run the commands:\n\n```bash\nnode cli/src/index.mjs compile docs/ontology --summary --json   # size, hash, kind census\nnode cli/src/index.mjs validate docs/ontology                   # problem files\nnode cli/src/index.mjs health docs/ontology                     # compile issues, cycles, unresolved\nnode cli/src/index.mjs mcp-verify docs/ontology --timeout-ms 15000\n```\n\nWhat the run must show is split into two contracts. `validate` reports **0\nproblem files**, `health` reports no structural compile/cycle/unresolved-edge\nerrors, and `mcp-verify` passes parser, server boot, every registered tool,\nstrict argument/enum checks, destructive dry-runs, batch no-write checks,\nbriefs, graph query smokes, and structured content checks. A cold-start or\nunqualified vault may still report `needs_attention`: `workspace_brief` and\n`agent_brief` must surface an invalid, unmeasured, or stale `meaningAssessment`\ninstead of relabelling it `healthy`/`ready`/`100`. The stronger `healthy` and\n`ready` result is reserved for a vault whose meaning assessment is current and\nwhose exact construction plan has passed the qualification and human-approval\ngate.\n\n## Recommended First User Flow\n\nFor a non-developer or a first-time AI-agent session:\n\n1. Install the macOS app and open the local vault folder there.\n2. Open App Settings → AI agent and check the setup/connection card.\n3. Read the root execution contract: `vault folder` sessions can use `.` as the\n   vault path, while separate `codebase root` sessions must pass the ontology\n   vault as an explicit absolute path.\n4. If the agent opens at a separate codebase root, copy the `agent-setup`\n   command before copying manual templates.\n5. Restart Claude Code, Codex, or Cursor.\n6. Run the read-first verification prompt or the JSON setup gate.\n7. Only then ask the agent to answer architecture questions or write ontology\n   updates.\n\nFor a developer terminal session:\n\n1. Run `ontology-atlas validate <vault>`.\n2. Run `ontology-atlas agent-brief <vault> --verify-fallbacks --json`.\n3. Run `ontology-atlas agent-brief <vault> --graph-db-pack`.\n4. Use follow-up commands before treating graph scans as evidence.\n",
docs/benchmark/results/2026-08-25-codex-B1-on.txt:1842:./src/entities/docs-vault/data/content.json:3:  "AGENT-MEMORY-POSITIONING": "# Agent Memory Positioning\n\n> Current product framing for launch, README copy, and prioritization.\n> Last updated: 2026-06-21.\n\n## One-line Position\n\n**A repo-native memory layer for Claude Code, Cursor, and Codex.**\n\nontology-atlas keeps a local, git-backed mental model of a codebase that AI\ncoding agents can read, query, and maintain through MCP.\n\n## The User Problem\n\nAI coding agents are useful, but they forget the durable structure of a\ncodebase between sessions:\n\n- which domains and capabilities exist\n- which files implement which capability\n- which dependencies matter before changing a module\n- which architectural decisions were already discovered\n- which documentation is canonical and which is stale\n\nWithout a shared memory layer, every agent session starts by re-discovering the\nsame project shape from source files and chat history.\n\nThe minimum supported path is intentionally plain: connect Atlas MCP or run the\nAtlas CLI from Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, or another coding agent, and the agent\nshould already receive a useful workspace brief, graph health check, handoff\npacket, and reviewable memory-diff workflow. No CodeGraph, Serena, language\nserver, grep wrapper, or external source-index service is required for the\nproduct to be usable.\n\nAtlas should not promise to replace code-reading tools. Built-in source search,\ngrep, language servers, Serena, CodeGraph, AST indexes, and similar tools are\ngood at structural facts: definitions, callers, imports, routes, and local\nimpact paths. Atlas helps the coding agent one layer above that: it preserves\nthe task starting point, product meaning, capability boundary, implementation\nevidence, and validation path that explain why those code facts matter.\n\n## Product Bet\n\nThe product is not valuable because it is \"an ontology editor.\" Developers do\nnot want another system they must manually maintain.\n\nThe product is valuable if it reduces the maintenance cost of AI-agent memory:\n\n1. Open a repo and generate an ontology draft automatically.\n2. Let the agent propose mental-model changes after real code work.\n3. Let the developer review those changes like a git diff.\n4. Make the next agent session visibly better because the memory is already in\n   the repo.\n\nThat loop is the product. The ontology graph is the implementation substrate.\n\nThe coding-agent value is therefore not \"Atlas reads all code for you.\" The\nvalue is \"Atlas tells the agent what to inspect, why it matters, and what must\nbe verified before acting.\" Store meaningful implementation evidence as\n`element` nodes and relations; leave exhaustive symbol graphs to deterministic\ncode-intelligence tools.\n\n## Why This Can Matter\n\nThe strongest positioning is:\n\n> Your AI coding agent forgets your codebase. Give it a local, git-backed memory\n> it can read and maintain.\n\nThis is stronger than:\n\n> Local-first ontology graph workbench with deterministic compiler and MCP tools.\n\nThe second sentence is technically accurate, but it leads with implementation\nlanguage. The first sentence leads with the daily pain of AI-assisted coding.\n\n## Success Conditions\n\nontology-atlas becomes a real product if a new repo can show value in one short\nloop:\n\n```text\ninit -> bootstrap -> agent answers better through MCP -> agent proposes sync\n-> developer reviews diff -> next task benefits\n```\n\nTarget: the first visible value should appear within 10 minutes.\n\n## Failure Conditions\n\nThe product fails if users feel they must become ontology maintainers before\nthey get value.\n\nRisk signals:\n\n- onboarding starts with manual node authoring\n- the hosted website feels like the primary product instead of intro/download\n- agent sync is not visible in git diffs\n- `health`, `workspace_brief`, and `maintenance_plan` explain problems but do\n  not lead to obvious next actions\n- bootstrap produces a graph that looks impressive but does not improve the\n  next agent interaction\n\n## What The Recent Engine Work Enables\n\nThe recent compiler/MCP/CLI work is meaningful because it supports the memory\nloop:\n\n- `compile_ontology` turns markdown frontmatter into a deterministic graph\n  artifact with `graphHash`, issue counts, aliases, pagination, and optional\n  indexes.\n- `query_ontology` lets agents ask graph-database-like questions without\n  pulling the whole vault into context.\n- `health` and `workspace_brief` give first-contact diagnosis before an agent\n  starts editing.\n- `maintenance_plan` turns graph cleanup into an ordered work queue.\n- Structured MCP schemas and error codes let agents recover from bad calls\n  instead of parsing fragile prose.\n- `analyze_repo_structure` and `infer_imports` reduce the cost of creating the\n  first draft from code.\n\nThis is the difference between a markdown graph toy and an agent-usable memory\nworkbench.\n\n## Agent Builder Concerns Lens\n\nUse this lens when deciding whether a new Ontology Atlas feature is worth adding.\nThe point is not to copy any agent framework UI. The point is to answer the\noperational problems that experienced agent builders keep designing around.\n\nCurrent research scan, 2026-06-05:\n\n- Anthropic's agent guidance stresses that simple workflows should stay simple,\n  while true agents should be reserved for open-ended loops that need tools and\n  environmental feedback:\n  <https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/building-effective-agents>\n- Claude Code guidance repeatedly treats context as the scarce resource:\n  project memory files, subagents, skills, and task-focused sessions exist to\n  keep the main agent context clean and durable:\n  <https://code.claude.com/docs/en/best-practices>\n  <https://support.claude.com/en/articles/14553240-give-claude-context-claude-md-and-better-prompts>\n  <https://support.claude.com/en/articles/14554000-claude-code-power-user-tips>\n- OpenAI's Codex and Agents SDK guidance puts persistent repo instructions,\n  issue-shaped prompts, MCP connections, approvals, and human-in-the-loop\n  resumption at the center of agent development:\n  <https://cdn.openai.com/pdf/6a2631dc-783e-479b-b1a4-af0cfbd38630/how-openai-uses-codex.pdf>\n  <https://openai.github.io/openai-agents-js/guides/mcp/>\n- Cognition's agent verification writing frames async coding-agent output as\n  incomplete until it includes end-to-end proof artifacts such as labeled\n  screenshots, videos, and pass/fail assertions:\n  <https://cognition.ai/blog/testing-development>\n- Google ADK frames agent infrastructure around structured context, sessions,\n  memory, tools, callbacks, traces, failures, and resumability:\n  <https://adk.dev/>\n- LangChain's memory work separates semantic, episodic, and procedural memory\n  so agents can distinguish durable facts, prior episodes, and reusable skills:\n  <https://docs.langchain.com/oss/python/deepagents/memory>\n- MCP frames agent integration as a typed boundary for tools, resources, and\n  prompts rather than an ad hoc pile of API calls:\n  <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/>\n\nTranslate those concerns into Atlas product tests:\n\n1. **Context selection**: Does the feature help an agent get the smallest useful\n   packet instead of flooding the context window?\n2. **Durable memory**: Does it turn an agent discovery into repo-backed markdown\n   that the next Claude Code, Codex, or Cursor session can reuse?\n3. **Tool and relation clarity**: Does it expose which MCP tool, graph query,\n   node slug, relation type, or source file the agent should use next?\n4. **Traceability**: Can a human see what changed, why it changed, and which\n   graph proof or validation command supports it?\n5. **Guardrails**: Does it fail closed before risky writes, stale assumptions,\n   ambiguous relations, or unverified graph rows become committed memory?\n6. **Human-in-the-loop economy**: Does it ask the developer for review at the\n   right point, not on every low-value observation?\n7. **Resumability**: If a session stops, can another agent reconstruct the\n   working state from the vault, git diff, graph health, and saved briefs?\n\nNear-term product implications:\n\n- Prefer \"agent-ready packets\" over long explanations: selected node profile,\n  blast radius, relation preflight, source file evidence, and next command in\n  one copyable bundle.\n- Make MCP connection and graph health visible as runtime state, not hidden in\n  documentation.\n- Treat draft ontology changes as first-class: staged, reviewable, discardable,\n  and backed by validation before they become durable memory.\n- Keep the visual UI quiet by default, but make proof, trace, and handoff\n  available through popovers, collapsible panels, and copy actions.\n- When adding a feature, ask whether it makes the next agent session cheaper,\n  safer, or more accurate. If not, it is probably decorative.\n\n## Positioning Guardrail\n\nUse \"ontology\" in technical documentation and architecture. Use \"repo-native\nmemory layer\" in launch copy and first-contact README text.\n\nThe marketable promise is not that users can draw a better graph. The promise is\nthat their AI coding agent stops starting from zero.\n\nWhen explaining the code side, use this boundary:\n\n```text\nBuilt-in search, grep, language servers, Serena, CodeGraph, and source indexes\nfind code structure.\nOntology Atlas preserves product/system meaning, implementation evidence, and\nthe next verification path for humans and AI agents.\n```\n",
docs/benchmark/results/2026-08-25-codex-B1-on.txt:1843:./src/entities/docs-vault/data/content.json:4:  "ARCHITECTURE": "---\ntitle: Architecture\ntags: [architecture, infra, overview]\n---\n\n# Architecture\n\n> 2026-07-27 update — the current architecture is a local-first, git-backed\n> **meaning layer**: a folder of markdown files that records what each part of\n> the product is, who owns it, what it depends on, and what proves it. People\n> and AI coding agents read and write that same folder. Round 10 permanently\n> removed every login and cloud-data screen. In today's route model, reading and\n> contextual writing happen together on Topology, ACP writes pause in the same\n> conversation for human review, and upkeep lives on the five-question Insights\n> page. The old `/ontology/studio` and `/ontology/edit` URLs only translate old links.\n> Earlier cloud and retired-workbench design notes are in `docs/archive/`.\n\n## High-level shape\n\n```\n┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│ User                                                    │\n│ ├─ /                       who is asking decides —     │\n│ │                          gateway face for a vault-   │\n│ │                          less web visitor, map for   │\n│ │                          the app and vault users     │\n│ ├─ /topology               map + contextual write     │\n│ ├─ /docs                   vault picker + editor       │\n│ ├─ /ontology               thin redirect → /topology   │\n│ ├─ /ontology/edit          compatibility redirect      │\n│ ├─ /ontology/studio        compatibility → topology    │\n│ ├─ /ontology/insights      five-question maintenance   │\n│ ├─ /git                    vault Git workbench         │\n│ ├─ /agents                 fetch · install · connect   │\n│ ├─ /projects               project list                │\n│ ├─ /project/[slug]         project detail              │\n│ ├─ /download               gateway as an explicit link │\n│ └─ /guide · /changelog     gateway reading (vault md)  │\n├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n│ App layer                                               │\n│ ├─ Next.js 16 App Router                               │\n│ ├─ next-intl /[locale]/ (en, ko)                       │\n│ ├─ output: 'export'  (static)                          │\n│ ├─ Tauri macOS shell (installed local workbench)        │\n│ └─ TaxonomyProvider · ToastProvider · MotionProvider   │\n├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n│ Data sources (mode-aware)                               │\n│ ├─ vault           Tauri native bridge → user disk      │\n│ │                  (source browser dev can use FSA)     │\n│ │                  (`src/features/docs-vault-local/`)  │\n│ └─ static          build-time dogfood manifest         │\n│                    (`docs/ontology/` → JSON import)    │\n└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n\n       ↑ stdio JSON-RPC\n\n┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│ MCP server (mcp/, v0.13.0)                              │\n│ ├─ read tools     connection/git proof · list/get/find ·│\n│ │                  compile_ontology · query_ontology ·  │\n│ │                  analyze_repo_structure · infer_imports│\n│ └─ write tools    add_concept · add_concepts ·          │\n│                    add_relation · add_relations ·       │\n│                    remove/replace relation · patch ·    │\n│                    reclassify · delete/rename/merge ·   │\n│                    absorb_document · git_snapshot ·     │\n│                    finalize_project_meaning             │\n│                                                         │\n│ AI agent (Claude Code, Cursor, …) reads/writes the     │\n│ same vault directory the user picked in /docs.         │\n│ compile_ontology builds the deterministic artifact;     │\n│ query_ontology answers graph-database-like questions    │\n│ over that artifact without introducing a server DB.     │\n└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n\n       ↑ stdio JSON-RPC (separate process)\n\n┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│ CLI (cli/, v0.11.0 — 54 commands)                      │\n│ ├─ init/agent-activity/add/import/list/find/validate/query│\n│ ├─ mcp-verify/analyze/infer-imports/bootstrap/compile  │\n│ ├─ preflight (commit preflight + pre-commit hook)      │\n│ └─ graph CRUD + deep dive commands                     │\n│                                                         │\n│ Developer-primary daily entry point. Same `.md` files. │\n└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\nThere is no backend, no server database, no login provider. The user's markdown\nfolder — called the **vault** everywhere in this repo — is the single source of\ntruth. Both the MCP server (used by the AI agent) and the CLI (used by the\ndeveloper) read and write that same folder.\n\nThe public app/website brand is **Ontology Atlas**. The macOS app bundle,\nbundle identifier, and DMG assets use the Ontology Atlas identity, while the\nrepo, CLI binary, and MCP package remain under `ontology-atlas`, so product\nnaming does not imply a backend, Firebase data dependency, or package rename.\n\nAtlas answers graph-database questions by computing the answer when asked. It\nnever keeps a second copy of the data. `compile_ontology` reads the frontmatter\n(the `key: value` block at the top of each `.md` file) and builds one graph file\nin memory — the same vault always produces byte-identical output, which is what\n\"deterministic\" means here. `query_ontology` runs its graph operations over that\nfile, and write tools save confirmed changes back into the markdown.\n\n## Surface contract — web and app\n\nDecided 2026-07-27 (`docs/DECISIONS.md`); the working rule agents load is\n`.claude/rules/surfaces.md`.\n\n**One codebase, one build.** The macOS app is not a port. Tauri loads the very\nsame static export into a WebView (`src-tauri/tauri.conf.json` →\n`frontendDist: \"../out\"`). Nothing is forked, and nothing should be.\n\n**What each surface is for.** The app is where the vault lives day to day: the\nplace a person reads the map, judges it, and connects their AI agents. The web\nhas two jobs, in this order. (1) It is the **gateway** — it opens the map with\nno install, for a demo, someone's first five minutes, or a shared link. (2) It\nis a **second-best workbench** where no app exists yet, which in practice means\nChromium on Windows and Linux, because the whole web surface needs the browser's\nFile System Access API to work at all. The order matters: every visitor we have\nobserved so far arrived through the web, so treating the gateway as merely \"the\nWindows stand-in\" would downgrade the only way people currently arrive. A\nWindows app would end job 2 on that operating system; job 1 never expires.\n\n**The two surfaces differ in a small set of modules — we call them bridges — not\nin the router.** Each bridge is the single file that calls a desktop-only\nability. The canonical roster is the bridge table in\n`.claude/rules/surfaces.md`; the ones that shape this document are:\n\n| Bridge | Module | Web behaviour |\n|---|---|---|\n| Vault absolute path | `src/shared/lib/tauri-vault-fs.ts` | FSA handle instead (no path) |\n| Git | `src/shared/lib/tauri-git.ts` | Cannot run → degraded card |\n| Keychain | `src/shared/lib/tauri-secrets.ts` | Impossible by design → degraded card |\n| LLM calls | `src/shared/lib/tauri-llm.ts` | Impossible by design → action not rendered |\n| In-app agent runtime (ACP) | `src/shared/lib/tauri-acp.ts` | A browser cannot spawn a process → one row states why and where |\n\nEvery bridge follows one convention: `getInvoke()` returns `null` when\n`isTauri()` is false, and the screen then says plainly that it cannot do this\nhere. Such a screen must tell the reader two things: **why** it cannot work here\nand **where** it can. New desktop abilities attach the same way. They do not get\na web version added later — that is a decision we have made, not a task waiting\nin a backlog.\n\n**What \"the same data\" actually guarantees.** The folder on disk is the single\nsource of truth, and both surfaces open the *physically same folder* — the web\nthrough an FSA handle, the app through an absolute path. The two surfaces cannot\nend up reading the same file differently, because one contract test runs the\nsame file through all three parsers (web, MCP, scripts) and the schema is\nwritten in exactly one place, `mcp/src/schema.mjs`. Anything that has to pass\nbetween the two surfaces is written **inside the vault** as plain text —\n`.ontology-atlas/activity.jsonl` and `llm-audit.jsonl`. That is the rule this\ncontract sets: *anything shared between surfaces lives in the vault folder.* If\ntwo people (or an agent and a person) edit at once,\n`patch_concept(expected_mtime)` stops the second write from silently erasing the\nfirst.\n\nThree things are deliberately **not** shared. The \"last opened vault\" handle:\neach surface keeps its own IndexedDB, so you pick the folder once per surface,\nand `/download`'s install step 02 says so. API keys: they live in the operating\nsystem's credential store, app only. UI preferences: localStorage. API keys and\npersonal display settings are not part of the shared model, so they stay out of\nthe vault.\n\n**Verification is split three ways** (this replaced the old web↔app round-trip\ncheck, which the same decision abolished). First, screens that both surfaces\nshare are tested once in the browser, and that counts as proof for the app too,\nbecause it is literally the same bundle — the exceptions are font rendering,\nscrolling, and the window frame, which still have to be checked in the installed\napp. Second, desktop-only abilities are proved *only* in the installed app.\nThird, the web surface has its own test, `tests/e2e/web-surface-smoke.spec.ts`,\nwired into `.github/workflows/e2e.yml` on a deliberately wider condition than\nthe rest of the suite — nobody watches the web by hand, so that test is its only\nguard.\n\nA deep link built on one surface is not guaranteed to rebuild the same screen on\nthe other. Where such links work, treat it as a convenience, not a promise.\n\n### In-app coding agents over ACP (2026-08-16)\n\nThe app can launch a coding agent the user already installed (Claude Code,\nCodex, …) and speak the Agent Client Protocol v1 to it over stdio. It is a\ndesktop-only ability, so it attaches through the bridge convention above. Both\ndecision records behind it are in `docs/DECISIONS.md`, dated 2026-08-16.\n\n| Module | Owns |\n|---|---|\n| `src-tauri/src/acp.rs` | Registry snapshot parsing, runtime detection (`ready` · `cli-missing` · `node-missing` · `uvx-missing` · `binary-missing`), launch resolution, isolated config + credential symlink, permission verdict, process-group termination |\n| `src-tauri/src/lib.rs` | The five Tauri commands (`acp_detect_runtimes`, `acp_start`, `acp_send`, `acp_stop`, `acp_permission_verdict`), the four `acp://*` events, and the vault-root rejection that also guards a session's working directory |\n| `src-tauri/src/acp-registry.json` | The committed registry snapshot, generated by `scripts/build-acp-registry.mjs` (`pnpm acp:registry`); icons are fetched at build time into `public/acp-icons/` |\n| `src/shared/lib/tauri-acp.ts` | The capability bridge and its web degradation. It never re-implements the permission policy |\n| `src/features/acp-session/` | `acp-client.ts` (JSON-RPC framing over the bridge) and `use-acp-session.ts` (one session's lifetime; a permission request blocks until the screen answers, and an unanswered one is refused) |\n| `src/widgets/acp-chat-panel/` | `AcpChatPanel.tsx` and `AcpPermissionCard.tsx`. **No route or control opens this panel yet**, so it is a module, not a surface |\n| `src/widgets/app-settings-menu/ui/AcpRuntimeSettings.tsx` | The one ACP surface a user can reach today: the settings sheet's Runtime section |\n\nThree properties are structural rather than incidental. The registry is a\ncommitted snapshot, so no ACP code path touches the network at runtime. A\nlaunched session runs against an app-owned config directory rather than the\nuser's global one, which is what makes the permission gate exist at all;\ncredentials are symlinked, never copied. And the child runs in its own process\ngroup, so quitting the app ends the adapter and everything it spawned.\n\n## FSD layers\n\n```\nsrc/\n├── app/        providers, init code\n├── views/      page-level components (1 view per route or group)\n├── widgets/    composite UI blocks (topology-map-v2, drawer, palette, …)\n├── features/   single interaction units (form, picker, search, …)\n├── entities/   business entities (project, ontology-class, …)\n└── shared/     UI primitives, lib helpers, config, types\n```\n\n**Import direction**: `app → views → widgets → features → entities → shared`. ESLint blocks the reverse.\n\nThe directory layout is enforced by `eslint-plugin-boundaries` in `eslint.config.mjs`.\n\n## Data flow\n\n### Vault graph lifecycle\n\n1. Markdown files are loaded from the vault folder.\n2. Each file's frontmatter becomes one **node** (a thing in the model: a\n   project, a domain, a capability, or an element), and the lists inside that\n   frontmatter become **relations** (typed links between two nodes).\n3. `compile_ontology` puts those nodes and relations into one fixed order,\n   resolves aliases, collects the problems it found, lists the frontmatter\n   arrays it would tidy up, computes a stable `graphHash` (one fingerprint for\n   the meaning of the whole graph), and optionally builds query indexes.\n4. `query_ontology` serves graph operations such as `neighbors`, `path`,\n   `project_scope`, `blast_radius`, `cycles`, `maintenance_plan`,\n   `workspace_brief`, and `health`. `agent_brief` accepts an explicit project\n   in multi-project vaults and derives a fresh categorical `meaningAssessment`\n   for that project; it does not reuse a saved score.\n5. Write tools change the markdown only when someone explicitly calls\n   add/patch/relation/rename/merge/delete. Analysis tools such as\n   `analyze_repo_structure` and `infer_imports` only propose candidates; they\n   never change a file.\n6. `finalize_project_meaning` runs after the writes are done. Once the vault\n   validates and the project's scope is complete, it saves a versioned record —\n   a *receipt* — of the competency answers, tied to the current graph and to\n   where the source code came from. Writing that receipt successfully does\n   **not** mean the source is still current. Three things stay separate and are\n   never added up into one number: whether the structure is complete, whether\n   each competency answer has evidence behind it, and whether the source has\n   been rechecked. If evidence is missing, out of date, or unresolved, the\n   assessment closes in one of the named review/evidence states instead of a\n   score.\n\n### Dual node identity\n\nEvery node carries two names. The `uid` is a lowercase UUIDv4 that never\nchanges — it is the node's permanent identity. The `slug` is the short readable\nname in the file path and the URL, and it can be renamed at any time. The\ncompiler requires UID validity and uniqueness,\nincludes UID and merge-owned `merged_uids` in the semantic hash, and exposes\n`uidToSlug`, `slugToUid`, and `mergedUidToSlug`. Compiled nodes and agent-facing\nnode summaries carry both values. Relations, adjacency maps, edge endpoints,\nURLs, and canvas IDs remain slug-based; exact MCP identity lookup and interop\nexports use UID. JSON-LD/GraphML serialize identity as `urn:uuid:<uid>`.\nRename/reclassify preserve UID; merge preserves the survivor and absorbs source\nidentity history. Invalid identity aborts compilation rather than producing a\npartial artifact.\n\n### Vault mode (user picked a markdown folder)\n\n1. `useLocalVault()` returns `{ status: 'loaded', handle, manifest }`.\n2. `useDataSourceMode()` returns `'local'`.\n3. `useProjects()` derives projects from `manifest.docs` (filter `kind: project`).\n4. `useOntologyInsight()` derives ontology nodes/edges from vault frontmatter.\n5. Mutations (`useProjectMutations.updateProject`) write directly to `.md`\n   files — the installed desktop app uses the Tauri native vault bridge,\n   hosted web uses the browser File System Access API directly (root-first-open,\n   2026-07: `FirstRunStarterModule` in the topology hub's INDEX panel calls\n   `useLocalVault().open()` on `/` itself, no install required). `/docs`'s own\n   separate local-source tab (browsing a second vault as a documentation\n   source inside that page) stays desktop-gated — that is a narrower, older\n   feature unrelated to opening your primary vault from the map.\n6. Ontology block import/export follows the same split: browser\n   `showDirectoryPicker()` or the Tauri `FileSystemDirectoryHandle` shim feeds\n   one recursive block reader/writer. Import plans conflicts before writing;\n   native or browser picker cancellation has no side effect.\n\nThe MCP server is independent: it reads the same vault directory through the\nfilesystem (Node.js `fs`), not the WebView bridge. AI agents and the installed app end up with the same view.\n\n### Static mode (no vault picked)\n\n1. `useLocalVault()` returns `{ status: 'closed' }`.\n2. `useDataSourceMode()` returns `'static'`.\n3. `useProjects()` derives projects from the build-time dogfood manifest\n   (`docs/ontology/` compiled into `src/entities/docs-vault/data/manifest.json`\n   by `pnpm docs-vault:build`).\n4. Mutations are blocked with a toast pointing to the vault picker.\n\nThis is what a first-time visitor sees: a real, filled-in ontology — this\nproject's own — before they have picked any folder of their own.\n\n## Routes\n\n```\n/                          shows a different screen depending on who opens it. A web visitor\n                           with no vault gets the gateway — the install-free intro page, the\n                           same view /download renders. A web user who has a vault, and anyone\n                           in the installed app, gets the map (or first run) unchanged. The\n                           installed app must never offer \"download this app\" to someone who is\n                           already running it. One function decides: isGatewaySurface() in\n                           shared/lib/nav-destination\n/topology                  map + contextual relation editor + change review. Any link labelled\n                           \"map\" or \"edit relation\" points here, not at / (gate:\n                           tests/contract/map-destination-route.contract.test.ts)\n/docs                      vault picker / editor / unified palette\n/ontology                  thin redirect → /topology?index=expanded (old tree/ego hub retired, B3)\n/ontology/edit             compatibility redirect → /topology contextual workbench\n/ontology/studio           compatibility redirect; translates node/mode/edit/via/review to /topology\n/ontology/insights         five-question maintenance board\n/git                       local vault git history / snapshot workbench (desktop-only destination)\n/agents                    coding agents this computer can run — the app launches them and\n                           you talk to them here. Fetches Node and the pinned CLI into an\n                           app-only folder when they are missing, runs the eight-step\n                           connection check, and repairs what it can. Moved out of the\n                           settings sheet 2026-08-20 (ledger 90): settings is where you pick\n                           values, this is operational work with progress state. API Key and\n                           Workspace stay in settings (the 2026-08-16 freeze and a different\n                           owning domain). Desktop launches the tools; on the web the page\n                           still renders and says what it cannot do, plus what it can.\n/projects                  project list (cards)\n/project/[slug]            project detail (inline edit when vault loaded)\n/project/[slug]/edit       full project editor\n/project/new               new project form\n/project/fallback          fallback page for missing slugs\n/download                  the same gateway page at its own address — it keeps the breadcrumb\n                           and the back-to-map link that / leaves out\n/guide                     the project guide, several chapters rendering docs/guide/*.md vault\n                           docs. Named `guide` and not `docs` because /docs is already the\n                           vault workbench (2026-07-30 ledger). Order and slugs live once in\n                           src/views/gateway-doc/model/guide-pages.ts\n/guide/[segment]           one chapter; static params come from that same registry\n/changelog                 renders docs/CHANGELOG.md from the vault, most recent sections only\n```\n\nAll routes are wrapped under `/[locale]/` by next-intl (en, ko).\n\n> Removed in earlier rounds: `/admin/*`, `/review/*`, `/diagnostics/*`,\n> `/knowledge/*`. Removed in Round 10: `/login`, `/signup`, `/account`,\n> `/reset-password`, `/settings/*`.\n\n**One piece of code decides which nav item is active; each screen size shows a\ndifferent list of buttons.** The desktop rail shows six destinations: Map, Docs,\nInsights, Projects, Agents, and Git. The mobile bottom bar shows four: Map,\nDocs, Insights, and Projects — contextual writing stays inside Map, while Agents\nand Git are desktop-only. Both read the same rules in\n`src/shared/lib/nav-destination.ts`, so every route belongs to exactly one\ndestination even on a screen size that deliberately hides that button. The\nretired\n`OperationsNav` and `OntologySubNav` are deleted. `AppSettingsMenu` mounts\nthrough the shell, while `AgentActivityChip` mounts in Topology's contextual map\ncontrols and `AgentActivitySettings` configures that current-work flow; none is\na navigation destination.\n\n## URL contract (query-param + node id grammar)\n\nA URL is how this app shares a view. A copied link, a handoff to an AI agent,\nand the browser's back button must all rebuild the same screen, so one node has\nto be written the same way everywhere. The **standard way to write a node id is\n`<kind>:<slug>`** — the kind in singular, a colon, then the slug, for example\n`capability:mcp-server`, `domain:views`, `project:ontology-atlas`. That is the\nvalue carried in `?node=` / `?p=` / `?realm=` and the id used in the text handed\nto an agent.\n\n### Query params\n\n| Param | Screen(s) | Meaning | Value shape |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `p` | `/`, `/topology` | focused/selected node | canonical `<kind>:<slug>` (bare slug tolerated) |\n| `open` | `/`, `/topology` | density-gate expanded parents | comma list of node ids |\n| `realm` | `/`, `/topology` | \"realm\" containment-subtree root | canonical `<kind>:<slug>` (bare slug promoted) |\n| `mode` | `/`, `/topology` | analysis mode | `overview` \\| `focus` \\| `path` \\| `health` |\n| `pathFrom` / `pathTo` (aliases `from` / `to`) | `/`, `/topology` | path source / target | node id |\n| `hub` · `c` · `impact` · `pulse` · `index` · `create` | `/`, `/topology` | focused hub · category · impact mode · pulse window · INDEX panel state · legacy create-node intent | see `src/views/home/model/url-state.ts` |\n| `workbench` · `edit` | `/topology` | contextual writer state · optional relation/target | `edit` \\| `create`; `edit=<relation>:<targetId>` |\n| `recent` · `ask` | `/`, `/topology` | recent-change lens · agent first-words intent | typed parsers in `src/views/home/model/url-state.ts` |\n| `via` | `/`, `/topology`, `/ontology` | origin marker for the return chip | `insights:<tab>` |\n| `review` | `/`, `/topology`, `/ontology/insights` | exact Do-next review row carried across handoff | stable review id, only meaningful with the matching handoff |\n| `node` | `/ontology` (redirect) | node to focus after redirect → `?p=` | node id (translated by `translateOntologyDeeplinkToTopologyParam`) |\n| `node` · `mode` · `edit` | `/ontology/edit`, `/ontology/studio` | legacy write deep link translated to `p/workbench/edit` | canonical and plural-folder node forms tolerated; `mode=create` → `workbench=create` |\n| `slug` | `/docs` | vault file to open | vault file path (`ontology/capabilities/foo`), not a node id — file paths are the docs vault's own address space |\n| `tab` | `/ontology/insights` | active maintenance question | `do-next` \\| `composition` \\| `connections` \\| `boundaries` \\| `freshness` |\n\n### One place builds node ids, one place reads them\n\n- **Building a link** (map · Insights · popovers · datasheets, all staying in\n  the map workbench): `buildTopologyMeaningEditorNodeHref` and\n  `buildTopologyMeaningEditorEdgeHref` in\n  `src/entities/knowledge-graph/lib/ontology-node-href.ts`. It normalizes any\n  input to canonical via `translateOntologyDeeplinkToTopologyParam`\n  (`capabilities/foo` → `capability:foo`; already-canonical / bare /\n  evidence-path pass through).\n- **Compatibility redirect**: `OntologyEditRedirectPage` normalizes legacy\n  `/ontology/edit` and `/ontology/studio` query values and replaces the route\n  with `/topology?p=...&workbench=edit` or `/topology?workbench=create`.\n- **Reading a link**: `parseHomeRouteState` owns `p/workbench/edit`, and\n  `parseOntologyStudioEditParam` remains only as the compatibility parser for\n  the relation/target pair. Unknown relation values open no editor rather than guessing.\n- **Turning a node id into a docs file path** happens in exactly one place: the\n  popover and the datasheet pass the focus model's `sourceSlug` (a path to a\n  file in the vault) straight into `buildDocsVaultHref({ slug })`. `/docs`\n  addresses files, not nodes, so its `?slug=` deliberately stays a file path.\n- Both the building and the reading side are pinned by `ontology-node-href.test.ts`,\n  `translate-ontology-deeplink.test.ts`, and\n  `url-state.test.ts`, and `OntologyEditRedirectPage.test.tsx`. Insights tab\n  serialization is separately pinned by `insights-tab-state.test.ts`.\n\n## Build pipeline\n\n```bash\npnpm docs-vault:build      # docs/ontology/*.md → src/entities/docs-vault/data/manifest.json\npnpm docs-vault:check      # verify committed docs-vault outputs are fresh\npnpm build                 # next build → static export → out/\npnpm vault:validate        # R11+ — frontmatter integrity + graph array drift\npnpm test:vault:validate   # focused validator CLI argument contract\npnpm vault:audit           # dogfood ontology capability/element paths exist in repo\npnpm test:vault:audit      # focused vault audit CLI argument contract\npnpm package:check         # MCP/CLI package files contract + self-test\npnpm test:contracts        # focused cross-package parser/schema/validator contracts\npnpm vault:migrate --list  # R11 — schema migration runner (dry-run default)\n```\n\nThe `docs-vault:build` step is automatic via `predev` and `prebuild` npm hooks. `docs-vault:check` also runs in the macOS release workflow, while `vault:validate`, `test:vault:validate`, `vault:audit`, `test:vault:audit`, and `package:check` remain explicit local/release-preflight gates.\n\n## i18n routing contract\n\n- In-app navigation: `Link`, `useRouter`, `usePathname` from `@/i18n/navigation` (locale-aware).\n- Locale-agnostic browser history: `useSearchParams` from `next/navigation` (no locale concern).\n- Cross-locale pivot (LocaleSwitch): raw `next/navigation` router, intentional.\n- Translation key namespaces: see `messages/{en,ko}.json` (kept in parity).\n\n## Test surface\n\n- **Vitest** (`tests/` + co-located `*.test.ts`) — unit + component\n- **Playwright** (`tests/e2e/*.spec.ts`) — visual regression, a11y, navigation, mobile\n\nE2E tests no longer rely on Firebase emulators (R10b removed firebase-tools\nand emulator scripts entirely).\n\n## Source-of-truth files\n\nWhen docs and code disagree, code wins:\n\n- `package.json` — what's installed, what scripts exist\n- `next.config.ts` — output mode, image config\n- `app/layout.tsx` — metadata, providers, head\n- `eslint.config.mjs` — FSD boundary rules\n\nLong-form docs:\n\n- [`AGENTS.md`](../AGENTS.md) — contributor guide (canonical for AI tools)\n- [`PRODUCT-DIRECTION.md`](./PRODUCT-DIRECTION.md) — mission\n- docs/benchmark/results/2026-08-25-codex-B1-on.txt:4245:./docs/ontology/capabilities/mcp-server.md:41:The complete proposal from `analyze_repo_structure` is not immediate write permission. The first call returns only the exact `reviewPlan`, plan/source digest, eight-stage status, remaining gap IDs, and maintains `canWrite:false`. An evaluator separated from the maker executes human owner CQ, current claim/citation, seven quality axes, full source-hidden tasks, cold-start, or previous CQ regression. The user must then approve this exact plan and gaps. Only upon resubmitting the same proposal and digest-bound `constructionQualification:v1` packet will the identical `writePlan` be revealed for rows seen initially. Source/plan drift, maker-only status, `not_measured`, red mandatory axis, regression failure, or unapproved gaps result in a fail-closed state. The `admission` within the same response classifies shadow-only states as `self_qualified`, `partial_visible_gap`, `human_review_required`, and `hard_block`. `self_qualified` is merely a signal of an automatic reflection candidate where all independent evidence passed; actual writes do not bypass existing human approval or digest-bound `writePlan` gates. Measured feature gaps appear in partial state, while policy/ownership/domain boundaries/conflicts remain for human review, and stale/unsupported/non-independent evaluations/source-hidden/regression failures remain hard blocks. Each qualification claim must include `proposalRefs` pointing to the exact `concept:`, `relation:`, `competency:`, and `impact:` rows of the current `reviewPlan`, with lifecycle `proposalCoverage` classifying missing/external proposal/source-hidden unverified rows as fail-closed. This is a receipt ensuring target matching for evaluator handoff, not a score or semantic judgment automatically approving the business truth of the claim.
docs/benchmark/results/2026-08-25-codex-B1-on.txt:4246:./docs/ontology/capabilities/mcp-server.md:43:Approval is declared provenance, not identity verification or a truth certificate. Project Markdown persists existing competency answers/witnesses/visible gaps; the finalizer receipt persists its body and current graph/source combination. Detailed CQ revision, axes, exact gap acceptance, and pre-write regression are execution evidence in MCP responses/agent transcripts and do not claim automatic restoration after restart. No new tools, kinds, sidecars, or writer tokens are created.
docs/benchmark/results/2026-08-25-codex-B1-on.txt:4247:./docs/ontology/capabilities/mcp-server.md:71:Cold-start semantic evidence is carried by existing `semanticEvidence` packets from classified current Markdown under root `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/`, `site/`, and `website/`. Explore up to 200 Markdown files and 1,000 directory entries across all three roots, stopping at 256 KiB pre-read for general semantic documents. Visit each actual path in the same directory only once; exclude archives and symlinks that are broken or outside the repository. Maintain the final packet limit of 6 docs and 1,200 chars/doc. For proposals arriving in the same call, recalculate existing read-only import receipts to verify TS/JS/Python file endpoints and Go importing-file/package-directory endpoints and directions. These documents and paths serve as evidence/provenance only; they do not auto-approve business meaning or `depends_on`. Without maker-independent qualification and human exact-plan approval, do not open `writePlan`. If relationship rationale directly names the exact repository `path:` of both endpoint concepts, that path must also appear in evidence for the same relationship. Fail closed with `relation-path-citation-mismatch` if only other documents are cited; do not block general semantic sentences by guessing filenames.
docs/benchmark/results/2026-08-25-codex-B1-on.txt:4248:./docs/ontology/capabilities/mcp-server.md:75:When a fresh `agent_brief` reads current source and incomplete competency together, set the first `nextActions` to `review_competency_repair`. The connected `meaningRepair:v2` compact manifest contains only disposition count, provenance, `reviewRevision`, and the first `meaning_repair_review` call; a separate `meaningRepairReviewPage:v1` carries current project Markdown declarations, structural review candidates from typed containment, canonical-path candidates directly supported by current source receipts, and all unresolved targets. Containment and path existence are not human semantic approval or action proof, so do not auto-convert candidates to `answered` or write them. If source/provenance/scope/validation/mtime is unstable, block repair while preserving existing source/health behavior. The first review read splits project, domain, and capability into deterministic, stateless cursors by their exact union. Each page has max 20 items, ≤5 KiB JSON, and provides literal `get_concepts(body:"full")` calls for the same target. `reviewRevision` binds graph/source/typed row/mtime; the verifier checks missing/duplicate/order/size/recency up to the last page, plus kind/mmtime/non-truncation of each full-body and source/bundle parity.
docs/benchmark/results/2026-08-25-codex-B1-on.txt:4249:./docs/ontology/capabilities/mcp-server.md:79:In Python cold start, read `README.rst`, non-executed static `setup.py` package contract, and top-level `__init__.py` package as bounded evidence. `infer_imports` condenses static imports from that package into file/module edges. This result is implementation evidence; do not auto-promote to domain/capability/semantic `depends_on`. Each module edge carries up to 5 exact file-edge receipts and presence of other evidence. Edges not in the vault return `rationale_review_required` instead of executable `proposedAction`. If the implementation path is already known, use `focusPath` or `reviewMode:"focus"` first. This mode returns exact incoming/outgoing static import counts (up to 100 receipts) and a stateless cursor for that file without a loadable vault. Empty results do not imply no impact; they fix symbol/test/dynamic behavior and ontology meaning as still pending separate confirmation. If `reviewMode` is omitted, return the existing full graph only when expected MCP total response (text + structured content) is ≤128 KiB. For larger reconciled scans, return only one review candidate, exact evidence, preflight calls for both concepts/relations, stop conditions, stateless cursor, and a `delivery` receipt with auto-selection reason/expected bytes instead of the full array. `reviewMode:"next"` explicitly requests this compact packet. `reviewMode:"full"` preserves the full shape but requires a second confirmation `allowLargeResponse:true` if exceeding 128 KiB. Oversized omission calls without a loadable vault for reconciliation return structured errors with two recovery options instead of faking safe packets. Internal analysis of `index_project` fixes explicit full to ensure this delivery default does not change existing plan meaning. Separate product/test code and value/type-only usage per evidence; calculate `productValueCount` (intersection of two-dimensional counts across all imports, not bounded samples) for module edges. Explicit type imports in JS/TS and imports inside Python's explicit `TYPE_CHECKING` guard are type-only. `value` means other static imports, not claiming runtime execution. If product code value usage is 0, do not hide test/type evidence but do not ask that import alone to approve product `depends_on`; require separate product meaning evidence. Additionally, the agent must read both concepts and direction, explain semantic rationale, and ask the human; record only one approved item with a non-empty `why`.
docs/benchmark/results/2026-08-25-codex-B1-on.txt:4250:./docs/ontology/capabilities/mcp-server.md:87:Rust repositories also do not report empty import graph as "no dependencies." `infer_imports.coverage` explicitly states that `use`/`mod`/macro dependency scan is unsupported when Cargo is detected, limiting the meaning of 0 edges to "no observed static imports" within supported languages. Instead, `analyze_repo_structure.configurationEvidence` and `index_project.configurationEvidence` preserve `[features]` declarations of root package or literal direct workspace members inside the repository, and literal `cfg`/`cfg_attr` feature predicates of conventional Cargo target sources as path/line/form/polarity/source role. This receipt does not evaluate predicates or execute build scripts/macros; it does not claim/write runtime impact, import dependency, or semantic `depends_on`. It does not hide workspace/package/feature/mapping/source limits or rejected members/predicates. Schema match in `relation_check` is also not semantic approval. New `depends_on` returns `approvalGate.writeAllowed:false` without executable `proposedAction`, written only after human explicit approval of observable capability, semantic rationale, and exact direction. The analyzer connects up to 12 element/path candidates for Python implementation boundaries participating in actual imports. Base on direct module/package boundaries, but allow security/policy/risk exact endpoints to reserve up to 2 slots to prevent risk ownership from being diluted in long import responses. Exclude unused files and conflicting flat slugs. `depends_on` proposed based on this path must match observed import direction to pass proposal validation. The model may select up to 4 exact import file endpoints outside auto-candidates for different exploration roles. The server does not auto-node these files; it fail-closes verifying only the proposal's exact path/limit/file-edge direction.

docs/benchmark/results/2026-08-25-codex-B1-on.txt:10106:src/entities/docs-vault/data/content.json-7-  "CHANGELOG": "# CHANGELOG\n\n> Major change history. Code commit messages answer *why*; this file answers *when / which surface changed*. Focused on **user-visible changes**, not PR-level granularity.\n>\n> Newest at the top. Entries stay date-based; the app version reached v1.0.0 on\n> 2026-07-27, so a release tag now carries the semver promise instead.\n\n---\n\n## 2026-08-25 · The released app carries its third-party notices\n\n- The macOS app and Windows installer now ship `NOTICE.md` and `LICENSE` inside the bundle. The app\n  statically links JavaScriptCore and WebKit through its compiled MCP sidecar, and ships the\n  Pretendard font, and both carry notice obligations that were not being met. Ontology Atlas itself\n  remains MIT; those licenses bind the component, not the program linking it.\n- The notice is generated from the actual dependency trees by `pnpm notice:build`, and\n  `pnpm notice:check` blocks a release whose notice has gone stale.\n\n## 2026-08-25 · A domain says when its description has fallen behind what it holds\n\n- A `domain` or `project` body is a summary of the nodes it contains. When that list changes and\n  nobody re-writes the description, the vault kept saying the old thing and nothing noticed:\n  every existing check asked whether the graph was intact, never whether it still told the truth.\n- `validate_vault` now reports `summaryFreshness`, and `query_ontology({operation:'maintenance_plan'})`\n  now raises a `rejudge_summary_membership` action, when a node's containment list changed after its\n  prose was last written. Both are advisory: nothing is blocked, no model is called, and no rewrite\n  is proposed. The body is a human judgement, so the vault asks for a judgement.\n- Outside a Git repository both report `checked: false` instead of a clean bill, because not looking\n  is not the same as finding nothing.\n\n## 2026-08-24 · Codex in-app chat pauses until Atlas MCP writes have an app-owned review gate\n\n- Installed-app acceptance proved that Codex `read-only` blocks direct file writes but can still\n  execute a self-registered Atlas MCP write without emitting the permission request the review card\n  depends on. The app no longer offers Codex in-app chat while that gap exists.\n- Codex remains detected and its external MCP connection/config path is unchanged. Claude Agent,\n  whose isolated session produces the app-owned permission checkpoint, remains available in chat.\n- Agent setup copy now scopes the no-port/no-network claim to the Atlas MCP child process and says\n  plainly that the coding agent itself may communicate with its provider.\n\n## 2026-08-23 · Selecting a node in the 3D dome lights its line to the top\n\n- In the dome view, pressing a node now lights the whole chain it hangs from (element to\n  capability to domain to the project at the apex) in the same indigo the selection already\n  uses, while the dome turns to face it. Pressing empty space lets it go. The flat map is\n  unchanged.\n\n## 2026-08-23 · The download page moves where the product is working\n\n- The evidence section now runs a short linked demo once it settles: three beats light a line of\n  the file on the right while the map on the left focuses the thing that line is (the node, its\n  domain, its dependency), then let go. Touching the map stops the demo immediately, and readers\n  with motion turned off simply get the resting section.\n- The agent scene's tool call arrives as typing, like a terminal writing, instead of fading in.\n  The text is still the measured session, unchanged.\n- The concept and relation counts under the map, and the file count in the evidence panel, count\n  up briefly when they first come into view.\n\n## 2026-08-23 · The gateway map labels only what the overview needs\n\n- The download page's evidence map no longer crams a label onto every name that fits. It labels\n  the hub, the seven domains, and the busiest capabilities (twelve names instead of\n  thirty-three), and every other concept stays as a dot until you hover or press it. All 82 dots\n  are still drawn, so the caption's count remains the picture's count.\n\n## 2026-08-23 · The demo clip loops without a control bar, and two sections read more plainly\n\n- The demo clip plays on a loop with no timecode or progress rail. It still pauses whenever the\n  section is off screen, and the play button remains for readers who turn motion off.\n- The evidence panel now shows only the lines of the file that carry meaning, and says how many it\n  left out. The four it drops are an id, a duplicate of the path above it, an empty list, and an\n  internal bookkeeping field.\n- The agents section says what the reader gets instead of naming a protocol: the app starts the\n  agent you already have, with no new key and no sign-in, and you approve a change before any file\n  moves.\n\n## 2026-08-23 · Every concept in the dogfood vault now has a Korean name\n\n- All 83 nodes carry a `display_ko`, up from 21. On a Korean screen the map, the\n  INDEX panel, node details, and the download page's evidence section no longer mix\n  English names into Korean text. Measured on `/ko/download/`: 33 labels drawn, none\n  Latin-only, where most were English before.\n- English screens are unchanged: they read `display_en` or the title as before.\n\n## 2026-08-23 · The evidence section shows one real file instead of counts\n\n- The right half of the download page's evidence section is now **one file that\n  actually exists in this repository**, together with what an agent reads out of\n  it. The kind counts and the sampled relation list are gone. A link opens that\n  file on GitHub so the claim can be checked.\n- None of those values are hand-typed: they are derived from the vault, and a\n  check fails when the committed copy and the vault disagree.\n- The section heading changed from claiming the map *is* this repository to naming\n  what actually happens: a few lines at the top of a file become the map.\n- Removed the caption under the agents section about agent writes ending up as a\n  line in a file.\n\n## 2026-08-23 · 시연 절이 가운데로 서고, 설명 문구가 실제 영상을 말합니다\n\n- 시연 절의 제목·설명·영상·캡션이 **한 축에 가운데로** 섭니다. 영상만 가운데이고\n  제목은 왼쪽이라 오른쪽이 비어 보이던 것을 바로잡았습니다.\n- **설명 문구가 없어진 장면을 말하고 있었습니다.** 「폴더를 고르면 지도가 되고,\n  에이전트가 그 지도를 읽고 씁니다」라고 적혀 있었는데, 지금 9초 클립에는 폴더를\n  고르는 장면도 에이전트도 없습니다. 실제로 보이는 것을 그대로 적었습니다.\n- 「무편집 · 무음」, 「한 테이크」처럼 만든 사람만 아는 말을 걷어내고 평범한 말로\n  바꿨습니다.\n\n## 2026-08-23 · 관문의 제목이 한 글자씩 쳐지고, 시연 무대가 자기 제목과 다시 맞습니다\n\n- 다운로드 페이지 제목이 커서와 함께 **한 글자씩 쳐집니다**. 한국어 1.14초, 영어\n  1.80초에 끝나며, 문장이 길수록 글자당 시간이 짧아져 어느 언어도 더 오래 기다리지\n  않습니다. 모션을 줄여 둔 사용자에게는 처음부터 문장이 다 보입니다.\n- 글자를 덧붙이는 방식이 아니라 자리를 미리 잡아 두고 잉크만 켜는 방식이라, 치는\n  동안 아래 내용이 한 픽셀도 밀리지 않습니다.\n- 시연 영상이 자기 절 제목과 **369px 어긋나 있던 것**을 바로잡았습니다. 세 절 중\n  이 절만 가운데 정렬이라 스크롤할 때 기둥이 끊겨 보였습니다. 영상 크기는 그대로입니다.\n\n## 2026-08-22 · 시연 영상을 영어로도 따로 찍었습니다\n\n- 영어 페이지의 시연 영상이 이제 **영어 화면 녹화**입니다. 화면 글자도 노드\n  이름도 영어이고, 한국어 페이지는 한국어 녹화를 그대로 봅니다. 「두 언어가 같은\n  녹화를 공유합니다」라는 안내 문구는 사실이 아니게 되어 지웠습니다.\n- 두 자산이 실수로 다시 한 파일이 되는 것을 막는 검사를 걸었습니다. 처음 이틀\n  동안 실제로 그 상태였는데 기존 검사는 전부 초록이었습니다. 둘 다 존재했고,\n  둘 다 선언한 길이였기 때문입니다.\n\n## 2026-08-22 · 관문의 시연 영상이 9초 한 장면으로 바뀌고, 한국어 화면에서 다시 재생됩니다\n\n- 시연 영상을 3분짜리 기능 투어에서 **9초 한 장면**으로 바꿨습니다. 이미 연결된\n  폴더에서 지도가 그려지고, 노드 하나를 누르면 이웃만 남으면서 그 아래·위·근거\n  문서가 함께 뜹니다. 폴더를 고르는 장면은 담지 않습니다. 컷도 배속도 없어\n  조립과 클릭이 실제 기계에서 걸리는 만큼 걸립니다. 자산도 12.3MB 에서 1.5MB 로\n  줄었습니다.\n- **한국어 페이지에서 시연 영상이 아예 재생되지 않던 문제를 고쳤습니다.** 영상\n  요소가 언어에 따라 다시 태어나는데 화면 진입을 감시하던 쪽이 버려진 요소를\n  계속 보고 있어서, 영어 페이지는 자동 재생되고 한국어 페이지만 포스터에 멈춰\n  있었습니다(2026-08-20 이후). 실측: `/en/` 2.97초 진행 · `/ko/` 0초 정지.\n\n## 2026-08-22 · 에이전트가 찾은 곳을 지도가 바로 보여 주고, 큰 지도는 한 번에 펼칠 수 있습니다\n\n- 지도 옆 에이전트가 Atlas의 `get_concept`를 호출하면 그 노드가 바로 선택되고,\n  `find_path`를 호출하면 정확한 최단 경로 노드와 관계선만 밝아집니다. 답변 문장을\n  추측해서 움직이지 않고 현재 볼트에 실제로 있는 typed tool input만 따릅니다.\n  Claude ACP가 입력을 뒤늦은 `tool_call_update`로 완성하는 실제 순서도 합쳐 읽습니다.\n- 상단에 「전체 펼치기」를 추가했습니다. 접혀 있던 containment 묶음을 모두 열고\n  전체 노드를 자동으로 화면에 맞춥니다. 다시 누르면 전체 접기로 돌아가며, 개별\n  `+N` 전개와 공유 링크의 `?open=` 동작은 그대로입니다.\n- 노드가 많은 지도를 여는 동안에는 큰 소개 문단 대신 화면 중앙의 회로형 스피너와\n  지금 하는 일 한 문장만 보입니다. INDEX 루트가 늦게 도착해도 내부가 기본으로\n  열리며, 사용자가 직접 닫은 루트는 존중합니다.\n- 노드 상세의 7개 액션 타일을 주 행동 하나와 「편집」·「더 보기」 메뉴로\n  정리했습니다. 중복 연결 합계, INDEX 하단의 에이전트 인계 푸터, 상시 관계선\n  범례, LNB 하단의 중복 활동 아이콘도 걷었습니다. 관계선 표본은 `?` 도움말에서\n  필요할 때 확인합니다.\n- 1024px에서 전체 펼치기 도구줄이 INDEX와 노드 상세을 침범하던 문제를 실측해,\n  1280px 아래에서는 오른쪽 두 번째 줄로 내리거나 상세이 열릴 때 숨깁니다.\n- 14인치에서 에이전트 패널과 노드 상세이 함께 열리면 상단 도구줄이 상세 폭을\n  제외한 남은 지도 중앙으로 자동 이동합니다. 에이전트 패널이 열린 동안 중앙·우측\n  버튼은 아이콘 밀도로 접혀 서로 겹쳐 보이지 않습니다.\n- 에이전트 패널이 지도를 줄이는 매 프레임에 전체·선택·영역·경로 중 지금 보던\n  상태의 카메라도 같은 클럭으로 새 가용 폭을 따라갑니다. 도크가 멈춘 뒤 지도가\n  마지막 목표에 속도 0으로 착지해 underdamped 되튕김으로 한 번 더 움직이지\n  않으며, 노드 상세을 닫을 때도 퇴장 중인 패널 폭을 최종 화면에 남기지 않아\n  전체 그래프가 한쪽으로 치우치지 않습니다.\n- 오른쪽 대화창은 창 높이를 통째로 덮는 벽 대신 위·아래·오른쪽을 12px 띄운\n  인셋 패널로 보입니다. 두 에이전트 갈래 모두 기존 지도 패널의 네 면 테두리,\n  radius, 표면색, 그림자를 공유하며 지도 폭을 양보하는 도크 동작은 그대로입니다.\n  패널 왼쪽 지도 컨트롤도 같은 seam에서 12px만 물러나 둘 사이 간격을 24px로\n  줄이고, 패널과 같은 260ms 동안 따라갑니다.\n- 대화창을 열 때 빈 검은 패널부터 보이던 순서를 바로잡았습니다. 헤더·폴더 안내·\n  추천·작성 칸은 도크 첫 프레임부터 최종 자리에 있고, 상태 배지의 작은 스피너와\n  「연결 중 → 준비됨」만 바뀝니다.
