게이트 구멍 다섯」 중 문서 토큰 무결성은 (12) 가\n닫았다. 나머지 넷을 실측으로 처리한 기록이다.\n\n### ① 라우트당 바닥 — 세웠다\n\n`hover-contrast.spec.ts` 는 19개 라우트에서 도는데 **비교 건수 바닥이 한\n라우트에만** 있었다. 나머지 18은 **0건을 비교하고도 초록**일 수 있었다 —\n「미달 없음」과 「아무것도 안 쟀음」이 화면에서 똑같이 생긴다. 자매 래칫 둘\n(`contrast-ratchet` `MIN_COMBINATIONS_PER_ROUTE=4` · `a11y-ratchet`\n`MIN_RULES_PASSED=15`)은 이미 그 문법을 쓰는데 이 계기만 안 쓰고 있었다.\n\n라우트별 실측(1512×900)에서 최소가 4라 **바닥을 3**으로 세웠다. 프로브 둘 다\n빨개진다: 바닥을 999로 올렸을 때 · 컨트롤 선택자를 망가뜨렸을 때.\n\n### ② 첫 뷰포트 제약 — 걷어냈다 (**+25건**)\n\n`r.top >= 0 && r.bottom <= innerHeight …` 한 줄이 **처음 보이는 화면 안**\n컨트롤만 재고 있었다. 그건 기술적 한계가 아니라 관성이었다 — Playwright 의\n`hover()` 는 대상을 알아서 스크롤해 넣는다.\n\n실측: **191 → 216건**. 정확히 census 가 센 뷰포트 밖 25개다. 새로 들어온 25건에\n미달은 없었다.\n\n### ③ 포인터 주차 — 방어로 넣되, **실측 손실은 0이었다**\n\n감사 자리가 *\"자기검증 테스트가 같은 페이지에서 두 번 훑는데, 둘째 스윕의\n쉬는 상태가 첫 스윕이 마지막으로 호버한 컨트롤 위에서 읽힌다 — 스윕마다 컨트롤\n하나가 조용히 누락된다\"* 고 지적했다. 구조적으로 맞는 지적이다.\n\n**그런데 재 보니 손실이 0이었다** — `/ko/` 에서 「치우지 않음 11 · 치움 11」.\n그 지적은 코드 구조에서 추론한 것이고 관측이 아니었다. `parkPointer` 는\n넣었지만(고정 좌표를 쓰지 않고 실제로 컨트롤이 없는 지점을 찾는다 — 이 파일이\n기록한 「(2,2)가 레일 위였다」 사고를 안 되풀이한다) **결함 수정이 아니라\n방어**라고 적는다. 안 그러면 다음 사람이 「고쳐진 버그」로 읽는다.\n\n### ④ 비텍스트 대비(1.4.11) — **재 보니 켜면 안 된다**\n\n호버로 보더가 바뀌는 자리를 전수해 3:1 로 판정했다: **60건 중 57건 미달**\n(1.12~2.92). 이걸 오류로 켜면 **57건 소음**이고, 그건 `design-gates.md` 가\n명시적으로 금지한 모양이다 — *\"수백 건 warning 을 만드는 룰은 강제가 아니라\n소음이고, 기존에 잘 돌던 신호까지 덮는다.\"*\n\n그리고 이 앱의 호버는 보더 하나로 상태를 말하지 않는다. 면·잉크·보더가 **함께**\n바뀌고, 「1px 보더는 이 어두운 바탕에서 무엇을 골라도 휘도로 못 가른다」는 것은\n2026-08-15 (10) 라운드가 이미 따로 실측했다. **「보더에 3:1 을 요구할 것인가」는\n계기의 질문이 아니라 디자인 판정**이라 그 자리로 넘긴다.\n\n**대신 사각을 문서화했다** — 그 스펙 머리말이 이제 「이 계기가 안 보는 것」 셋을\n숫자와 함께 적는다(비텍스트 · `group-hover` 자식 잉크 25자리 · 볼트가 있어야\n그려지는 컨트롤).\n\n### 이 라운드가 스스로 밟은 함정 하나\n\n첫 census 가 `judgeAdjacentMarks(a, b)` 를 **위치 인자로** 불렀다. 그 함수는\n객체를 받으므로 전부 `null` 을 돌려줬고, 「60건 판정 · 미달 0」이라는 **공짜\n초록**이 나왔다. 그 숫자를 믿었으면 「켜도 소음 0」이라는 정반대 결론으로 갔을\n것이다. 객체로 고쳐 부르니 57건 미달이었다.\n\n> **판정 함수가 `null` 을 돌려주는 것과 「통과」는 다르다.** 이 저장소는 그\n> 계약을 `contrast.mjs` 안에 이미 적어 뒀는데(*\"못 읽은 색은 «통과» 가 아니라\n> «미측정» 이다\"*), 부르는 쪽이 그걸 안 지키면 같은 사고가 난다.\n\n### 남은 것\n\n`group-hover` 자식 잉크(25자리)와 조건부 컨트롤(≈58%)은 여전히 사각이다. 후자의\n처방은 (10) 이 이미 적었다 — **런타임 계기 확장이 아니라 소스 층의 계산 계약**.\nCDP `CSS.forcePseudoState` 로 기존 채집기를 호버 상태 위에서 돌리는 길은 조사만\n해 뒀고(이 저장소 사용 0건), 전자를 푸는 데는 그게 맞는 도구다.\n\n**서명 (accountable)**: jinan\n**상태**: 유효\n\n---\n\n## 2026-08-15 (9) — 비표준 구현 루트도 의미 후보의 증거가 되되, 기능으로 자동 승격하지 않는다\n\n**선행 기록**: 의미 분석은 사업 서술과 구현 증거를 교차 확인한 제안만 내고, 폴더\n구조만으로 사업 능력을 확정하지 않는다는 기존 결정들을 유지한다. 이번 held-out\n측정에서 기존 분석기는 `lib/`와 함께 존재하는 `internal/` 구현 루트를 읽지 못해,\n제품 서술이 있어도 구현 witness가 끊기는 반증 조건이 관측됐다.\n\n**관찰된 현상**: 대규모 저장소의 실제 구현이 `internal/<role>/` 아래에 있지만\n분석기의 첫 번째 source root 선택 때문에 해당 경로가 semantic candidate에 연결되지\n않았다. 한 저장소는 4개 요소·사업 후보 0개로 반환됐고, 재측정 전에는\n`internal/bundler`가 분석 결과에 없었다.\n\n**사용자 문제**: 개발자 또는 코딩 에이전트가 저장소를 처음 분석하는 순간, README의\n제품 목적과 실제 구현 위치를 함께 따라갈 수 없어 후보를 재탐색하거나 수동으로\n맥락을 복구해야 한다. 다만 `internal`이라는 이름 자체는 구현 계층일 뿐 사업\n의미의 증거가 아니므로, 이를 capability로 올리면 기존의 폴더=사업 오탐을 되살린다.\n\n**PO pass**: `internal/`의 직접 하위 디렉터리를 bounded implementation element로\n수집하고, 신뢰 가능한 제품 문장과 같은 의미의 구현 경로가 함께 있을 때만 기존\nproposal-only 후보를 만든다. 새 writer·MCP/CLI 필드·vault 스키마·자동 write는\n추가하지 않으며, 단위·held-out·무쓰기 검증으로 확인한다.\n\n**결정**:\n\n- `internal/`은 기존 `src/source/lib/app`를 대체하지 않는다. 다른 source root가\n  선택된 저장소에서도 `internal/`의 직접 하위 디렉터리를 추가로 최대 48개까지\n  요소 증거로 수집한다. `internal/`만 있는 저장소에서도 동일하게 동작한다.\n- 수집된 항목은 `elements/<role>`과 실제 상대 경로만 가진다. `internal/<role>`을\n  `capabilities/<role>`로 만들지 않으며, 하위 깊이 전체를 재귀 스캔하지 않는다.\n- 신뢰 가능한 mission/product/package 문장에 web/JavaScript/TypeScript bundler\n  또는 bundler project가 명시되고, 구현 요소 경로에 bundler/builder/compiler/linker\n  witness가 있을 때만 `capabilities/build-tooling`을 기존 proposal-only 형식으로\n  제안한다. 제안은 여전히 사람 의미 승인 전에는 vault에 들어가지 않는다.\n- 기존 `canWrite: false`, human approval, visible gap/review question 경계를\n  유지한다. 이번 slice는 분석 증거 수집과 후보 연결만 바꾼다.\n\n**기각한 대안**: `internal`의 폴더명을 곧바로 capability로 올리는 방식은 사업\n의미와 구현 구조를 혼동하므로 기각했다. `internal`을 source root 우선순위로만\n바꾸는 방식도 `lib`와 `internal`이 공존하는 저장소에서 한쪽 증거를 잃으므로\n기각했다.\n\n**반증 조건**: ① 서술과 경로가 모두 없는 저장소에서 `build-tooling`이 제안되거나\n`internal/<role>`이 capability로 반환되는 사례가 1건이라도 나오면 이 결정은\n실패한 것이다. ② 48개 제한을 넘긴 내부 루트에서 제한·누락이 표시되지 않으면\nbounded scan 경계가 실패한 것이다. ③ 독립 source-hidden 평가에서 구현 경로가\n늘었지만 다음 작업 handoff 또는 후보의 의미 판단이 개선되지 않는다면, 다음\n라운드는 clue 추가가 아니라 evaluator/질문 품질부터 다시 연다.\n\n**루브릭**: 21/24 (Problem insight 4 · User moment 4 · Differentiation 3 ·\nOntology value 4 · Agent value 4 · Verification 2, 치명적 0 없음). Verification 2는\n이번 기록 시점에 source-visible calibration과 regression은 완료했지만,\nsource-hidden 독립 evaluator의 qualification은 아직 완료하지 않았기 때문이다.\n\n**검증 증거**: analyzer unit 91/91 통과. held-out 3개 저장소 재측정에서 기존 후보가\n없던 대규모 저장소는 31개 bounded 요소와 `capabilities/build-tooling` 제안을 얻었고,\n다른 두 저장소는 새 후보를 만들지 않았다. 전체 MCP/app dogfood와 source-hidden\nqualification은 이 결정의 후속 조건으로 남긴다.\n\n**상태**: 유효 · bounded implementation-evidence slice 집행 · source-hidden\nqualification pending\n\n---\n\n## 2026-08-24 — Tauri runtime hardening: keep only what is used, and make what could not explain itself explain itself\n\n**Prior record**: the 2026-07-27 surface contract established that the app is the\nvault's home and that Tauri carries one static build. This decision does not move\nthat boundary. It removes permissions that were never exercised and adds a\ndiagnostic that did not exist.\n\n**Observed phenomenon**: (1) `tauri` sat at 2.11.2 and `tauri-build` at 2.6.2,\nthree patches behind. (2) Nothing prevented a second instance against one vault —\ntwo watchers, two ACP harnesses, two MCP sidecars, and two writers on the same\nMarkdown were all reachable, and the updater's restart makes a second launch a\nroutine event rather than an exotic one. (3) Every `println!` among 16,562 lines\nof Rust sits inside `#[cfg(test)]`. A packaged `.app` sends stdout nowhere a\nperson can reach, so an installed build left **no evidence at all** behind a\nreport of \"the agent stopped\". (4) The CSP was byte-identical to the example block\nin the Tauri v2 CSP documentation — a page whose own text says \"tailor this to\nyour own application needs\". `asset:` was inert because `assetProtocol` is not\nenabled, and `customprotocol:` is a v1 relic referenced nowhere in this\nrepository. (5) `core:default` granted `core:image`, `core:resources`,\n`core:menu`, and `core:tray` to a webview that never called them.\n\n**User problem**: an owner whose installed app misbehaved had nothing to send.\nAnd in a product whose files are the source of truth, concurrent writers are the\none failure it cannot absorb.\n\n**Decision**:\n- Move to `tauri` 2.11.5 and `tauri-build` 2.6.3. `Cargo.toml` already carries a\n  caret, so only the lock changes.\n- Register `tauri-plugin-single-instance` **first**, per its own guidance. A\n  second launch hands focus back to the window that already exists.\n- Add `tauri-plugin-log`: one rotating 5 MiB file in the OS log directory. It sits\n  outside the vault so it never becomes a second store of meaning, and stays at\n  `Info` so it records **what the app did** — never vault content, prompts, or\n  secrets. `llm_audit.rs` still owns the LLM transfer ledger; this does not\n  replace it.\n- Delete the inert `customprotocol:`, `asset:`, and `http://asset.localhost`\n  entries from the CSP.\n- Narrow the capability from `core:default`'s nine sets to five. Only two have an\n  observed frontend caller — `listen` needs `core:event`, `getVersion` needs\n  `core:app`. `core:path`, `core:window` and `core:webview` are retained, not\n  because they are known to be used, but because narrowing a permission without\n  installed-app proof fails silently, and a capability gate cannot catch that.\n\n**Rejected alternatives**: adopting `tauri-plugin-fs`, `dialog`, `shell`, `http`,\nor `store` was rejected. Exposing filesystem and network permissions to the\nwebview is harder to prove than the present shape, where 48 narrow Rust commands\nare the only door. Enabling the asset protocol was rejected too — nothing uses it.\n\n**Where the line sits on paths**: the vault's own root path is logged when the\nwatcher starts, because a watcher that never fires cannot be diagnosed without\nit. Individual note filenames are not: `notify`'s error `Display` embeds the paths\nit was watching, so watcher failures log the error *kind* only. In this product a\nfilename is already meaning, and the log is the one place meaning is not kept.\n\n**Falsifiers**: (1) one line of vault body, note filename, prompt text, or a key\nappearing in the log file means this decision failed. (2) If the narrowed capability silently kills\nany webview ability, restore `core:default` and record why. (3) If single-instance\nblocks a legitimate second launch — someone opening a different vault\nconcurrently — reopen the instance boundary at vault granularity rather than app\ngranularity.\n\n**Verification evidence**: `cargo check` clean; `cargo test` 210 existing tests\npass; 8 added window-geometry units pass. No installed-app evidence exists at the\ntime of this record; it is bound to the window decision below.\n\n**Status**: valid · installed-app evidence pending\n\n---\n\n## 2026-08-24 — The `1512×982` window default was never a window. It was the whole display\n\n**Prior record**: the 2026-07-27 record gave the workbench seat the 14-inch first\nviewport, window lifecycle, and wide-screen density. This is that seat's ruling.\n\n**Observed phenomenon**: the `1512×982` declared in `tauri.conf.json` is the\n**entire logical display** of the 14-inch MacBook Pro. Tauri's `width`/`height`\nare the inner content size, so 982 asked for 982 plus a 28pt title bar inside a\n945pt visible frame, and AppKit silently constrained it on every launch. The\nledger's own measurements read `1512×949` outer and `1512×917`/`918` content:\n**982 has never once been observed as a window.** Meanwhile `measure-a11y.mjs`,\n`measure-graph-readability.mjs`, and `measure-contrast.mjs` have always swept\n`1512×900`. The shipped first viewport and the measured first viewport have never\nbeen the same number.\n\n**User problem**: an owner who sized the window lost that size on every relaunch,\nand the viewport our instruments testify about was not the one anyone sees.\n\n**Decision**:\n- Change the default height from `982` to `900`. 900 + 28 = 928 ≤ 945, so the\n  shipped window is honoured instead of constrained, and the number becomes the\n  one the measurement scripts already use. Width stays 1512 so that every `1512`\n  measurement in the ledger describes a viewport the app actually opens.\n- Adopt `tauri-plugin-window-state` with `SIZE | POSITION | MAXIMIZED` only.\n  `FULLSCREEN` is not saved: restoring it performs a Space transition before first\n  paint and gives macOS's own restoration a second owner. `VISIBLE` is not saved:\n  it can produce a launch with no window, which a person cannot distinguish from\n  the app failing to start. `DECORATIONS` is not saved: this app never changes\n  them, so saving only adds a route to an undecorated window that cannot be moved\n  or closed.\n- Run `sanitize_window_geometry` on **every launch**, restored or not. The plugin\n  restores size unconditionally in **physical** pixels, and its off-screen test\n  guards position only — and passes when any single corner intersects a display.\n  Quitting at 1512×900 on a 2× panel writes 3024×1800; relaunching without that\n  display would otherwise ask a 1× panel for a window larger than itself. The\n  sanitizer converts to logical points, clamps, and recentres when fewer than\n  120pt of grabbable title bar remain on screen. It **accepts permissively and\n  places conservatively**: acceptance and the height ceiling use the 24pt menu\n  bar every non-notched panel spends, while a recentred window is placed below\n  the notched 37pt. The first version used 37 for both, which called a window\n  snapped to the top of any external monitor unreachable and recentred it on\n  every launch — the plugin restored the owner's window and the sanitizer\n  immediately took it away again. That is this decision's own falsifier (1),\n  and it was in the code before an audit found it.\n- Do **not register** the plugin under the verification harness. Skipping the\n  initial restore is not enough — the write on exit must also be impossible, or a\n  harness run overwrites the owner's real geometry. `--reset-window-state` lets\n  the release preflight run without saved geometry. A gate whose verdict depends\n  on the last window drag is not evidence.\n\n**Rejected alternatives**: dropping the width to 1440 was rejected — every\nexisting `1512` measurement in the ledger would then describe a viewport the app\nnever opens. Clamping only on the restore path was rejected too: on the smallest\ndisplay this product promises, 1440×900, a 900pt content area does not fit even\nwithout restoration.\n\n**Falsifiers**: (1) if a user reports the window opening somewhere they did not\nleave it, or a `--min-window-size` failure traces to the state file rather than to\n`tauri.conf.json`, the sanitizer is wrong — and the retreat is dropping `POSITION`\nto restore `SIZE` only, not removing the clamp. (2) If the default reads as\nmaximized, move the width to 1440 and re-anchor the measurement scripts in the\nsame change.\n\n**Verification evidence**: 8 `sanitize_window_geometry` units pass — identity (a\nfitting window is returned untouched), oversized clamp plus recentre, 2× physical\ngeometry restored onto a 1× display, a title bar above the menu bar recovered, a\nwindow dragged off the right edge recovered, below-minimum raised, and a\nleft-hand external display with a negative origin preserved. A\n`check-desktop-readiness.mjs` gate now asserts the shipped window fits the\n14-inch reference panel and that `minWidth`/`minHeight` equal the Rust constant;\nreverting the height to 982 turns it red. **No installed-app measurement exists\nyet** — deleting the state file and measuring first launch, then resize and\nrelaunch, on a 14-inch panel is this decision's outstanding condition.\n\n**Measured on the installed app, 2026-08-24** (16-inch panel, logical 1728x1117,\n2x scale; ad-hoc signed build of this branch):\n\n1. First launch with no saved state opened at exactly **1512x900**, unconstrained\n   — `fit source=default requested=1512x900 applied=1512x900 recentered=false`.\n   The old 982 could not produce this; the ledger had only ever measured 949.\n2. Quit and relaunch returned the window to the same rectangle, and the line read\n   `source=restored`, so the diagnostic separates a restored window from a default\n   one as designed.\n3. The saved file confirmed the hazard this decision was written around: a\n   1512x900 logical window is stored as **`\"width\": 3024, \"height\": 1800`** —\n   physical pixels, exactly as predicted.\n4. **The clamp did not work, and only the installed app could show it.** A planted\n   3000x2000 state produced a 3000pt window hanging off the display while the fit\n   line reported `current=1512x900 recentered=false`. `set_size` is dispatched\n   through the event loop, so reading the window back straight after a restore\n   returns the *pre-restore* geometry — the clamp was a no-op in the one case it\n   exists for, and all 12 unit tests passed throughout, because the pure function\n   was never wrong. The wiring was.\n   The fix inverts it: the plugin no longer performs the initial restore\n   (`skip_initial_state`), this app reads the saved file itself, sanitises the\n   numbers **before** applying them, and never reads the window back. Re-measured\n   with the same planted state: `requested=3000x2000 applied=1728x1065\n   recentered=true`, window on screen at 0,37. 1065 is exactly\n   `1117 - 24 - 28`.\n\nThat fourth item is the entry's own lesson: a green unit suite proved the\narithmetic and said nothing about whether the arithmetic ever ran.\n\n**Status**: valid · measured on the installed app\n\n---\n\n## 2026-08-24 — PO Council: reject the `ontology-atlas://` OS URL scheme (0/24)\n\n**Convened because** an OS-registered URL scheme is a public contract change, and\nlinks minted into portable vault Markdown outlive any retreat. Round 1 ran as five\nparallel independent seats; Round 2 completed for Craft, Steward, and Wedge.\nEvidence and Leverage stand on their Round 1 positions, both already blocking. No\nclaim is made that all five returned rebuttals.\n\n| Seat | Verdict | Signed rows |\n|---|---|---|\n| Evidence | Do not build | Problem insight 0 · User moment 0 |\n| Craft | Do not build (changed in Round 2) | Verification 0 |\n| Steward | Do not build | Ontology value 0 · Agent value 0 |\n| Wedge | Do not build | Differentiation 0 |\n| Leverage | Do not build now | appetite 0 this cycle |\n\n**Rubric**: 0/24 (fatal zeros: Problem insight, Ontology value, Agent value,\nVerification)\n\n**Observed phenomenon — none**: zero third-party issues, roughly 6 measured\ndownloads, and 35 of 35 observed visitors arriving through the web. The population\na scheme would serve is effectively empty. The motivating ledger record the\nrequest cited was resolved in-band the same day; its defect class is *a prescribed\naction name with no executor*, and its recorded cure was **typed executors in the\nsame channel** — the opposite of a second, untyped, fire-and-forget address\nvocabulary.\n\n**Decisive facts**: (1) putting a uid in a URL is already prohibited by\n`mcp/README.md:466` and spec §4 — \"UID is not a … URL token; Slug remains the\ninspectable address\". (2) A uid link is invisible to `findBacklinks`, and\n`defaultUrlTransform` reduces it to an empty string so it renders as a **dead\nspan inside Atlas's own Markdown viewer**. (3) A bare uid names no vault. (4)\n`is_openable_url` in `src-tauri/src/lib.rs` already records the mirrored\n**outbound** judgment, allowing only http/https — registering an inbound scheme\nwould put the product on both sides of one recorded threat model. (5) No CI lane\ninstalls a bundle to `/Applications`, so the only available gate is either a\nstring-pinning test that stays green while the feature is broken, or a human\nremembering to open a terminal after each release.\n\n**Instead — one repair, passed separately**: `mcp/src/ontology-engine.mjs:1522`\nhands agents a retired `/ontology/studio/?node=` href today. That is the one live,\nfile-observed instance of the requester's own defect class, found independently by\nthree seats. Change it to the canonical `/topology/` focus address, pair\n`mcp/README.md` per the documentation table, and add a contract test banning\nretired namespaces in MCP-emitted hrefs.\n\n**Gate blindness found in passing**: `scripts/check-decision-record.mjs` watches\nonly added or deleted `app/` routes and two `CONTRACT_FILES`, so registering a\nscheme in `tauri.conf.json` would pass `decisions:check` green. Recorded as a\n`/gate-probe` candidate.\n\n**Recorded dissent**: slugs are mutable and the uid is the immutable identity, so\na rename-durable address is a real gap; and the cold-start moment — app closed,\nconcept reference sitting in an external transcript — is the one moment no in-band\nchannel serves, which makes waiting for observation necessarily one beat late.\nSteward conceded the durability gap is real and named the follow-up it would score\npositively: teach the existing `?p=` resolver to accept uid and `merged_uids` and\ncanonicalize to `kind:slug`, as a separate slice with its own pass.\n\n**Falsifier**: within one dogfood week, or in the first measured installed cohort,\nthree or more occasions of hand-searching the map because the app was closed or\nopen on another vault; or a third-party issue asking for a clickable app link; or\na named client that linkifies non-http schemes for a real user. Any of these\nreopens the question — and only then, slug plus vault-qualified, focus-only,\nallowlist-parsed, with a threat model and a new record first.\n\n**Slice**: IN nothing · OUT scheme registration, uid addresses, write-capable or\naction-executing URLs, any second address vocabulary in vault Markdown ·\nappetite 0\n\n**Status**: valid · human signature pending\n\n---\n\n## 2026-08-24 — PO pass: hold `tauri-plugin-notification` for ACP completion and blocked signals (10/24)\n\n**Prior records**: the 2026-07-27 update-notification pair (6/24 do-not-build,\nstanding; the same-day owner overturn was updater-specific on a cost-side change\nand does not transfer), the 2026-08-01 quiet-threshold and work-unit inbox\ndecision, (23) 2026-08-17, and (92) 2026-08-21.\n\n**Observed phenomenon**: `acp.rs` and `lib.rs` emit `acp://message|stderr|notice|exit`\nto the webview only; no OS-level signal exists. But **no recorded instance** exists\nof anyone missing a completion or an `allow_once` prompt, and this checkout holds\nno `.ontology-atlas/` transcripts to check either way.\n\n**Sharpest sub-case**: a session blocked at `allow_once` emits no further work\nunits. Under the standing 2026-08-01 rule that judges completion by five minutes\nof quiet, a **blocked** session and a **finished** one are indistinguishable\ninside the app's own inbox. If that misclassification is real, the first repair is\nin-band — teach the inbox to separate blocked from quiet — not an OS plugin.\n\n**Rubric**: Problem insight 2 · User moment 2 · Differentiation 2 · Ontology value\n0 · Agent value 2 · Verification 2 = **10/24** (fatal zero: Ontology value)\n\n**Escalation**: mechanically triggered but not convened, citing the standing\n2026-07-27 interpretation that convening a council to reach \"do not build\" is\nprocess theatre. If investigation flips the direction to build, a fresh pass is\nmandatory and escalates on its own numbers.\n\n**Decision — investigate first, no code**: three real dogfood ACP sessions. For\neach, record whether a write waited at `allow_once` while the app was not\nfrontmost, how long until the person noticed, and whether the in-app inbox\npresented that blocked session as quiet or done. If the misclassification is\nobserved, the smallest slice is the inbox distinguishing blocked from finished;\nonly its measured insufficiency reopens the OS plugin.\n\n**Pre-committed no-gos if it ever builds**: no vault-derived text in notification\nbodies, because a lock screen is not the vault; opt-in; the notification focuses\nthe app and executes nothing; and the OS layer never becomes a second store of\nsession truth — the inbox stays canonical.\n\n**Recorded dissent**: an app with no interrupt path makes the person the polling\nloop, and the blocked-agent case is heavier than mere awareness because the\n**agent's** work stalls too. Building only after observation is necessarily one\nbeat late.\n\n**Falsifier**: a single dogfood session where an `allow_once` request waited past\nthe five-minute threshold because nobody was looking disproves this hold on the\nspot.\n\n**Status**: valid · three investigation sessions pending\n\n---\n\n## 2026-08-24 — MCP handed agents a retired address; one repair, and a gate so it cannot return\n\n**Prior record**: the 2026-08-24 council that rejected the `ontology-atlas://`\nscheme named exactly this repair as the thing to do instead, \"as a separate\nordinary change with its own routine pass\". This is that pass. Standing decision\n(92), 2026-08-21 owns what `/ontology/studio?node=…` translates to.\n\n**Observed phenomenon**: `mcp/src/ontology-engine.mjs:1522` emitted\n`href: /ontology/studio/?node=<focusParam>` from `builder_context` — a **retired\nlegacy redirect**. The vault's own `docs/ontology/elements/ontology-edit-redirect.md`\nsays of those addresses: \"neither old address is a navigation destination or write\nsurface\". The MCP server was contradicting the ontology it exists to serve, in the\none field that tells an agent where meaning is edited. Three council seats found\nit independently. A sweep of `mcp/` and `cli/` confirmed it was the only navigable\naddress either package emits.\n\n**User problem**: an agent finishes a `builder_context` read and hands its human\n\"open the Workshop here\". The address works only because a client-side redirect\ncatches it — which is the requester's own defect class, a prescribed action whose\nexecutor is somewhere else, found alive in shipped output.\n\n**Decision**: emit `/topology/?p=<focusParam>&workbench=edit`. That is byte-for-byte\nwhat the redirect already resolves to under decision (92).5, so the destination does\nnot change and the hop disappears. `focusParam` is untouched: it is already the\ncanonical graph node id (`kind:tail`) the map's `?p=` resolver exact-matches, and\n`mcp/README.md` documents its round-trip as the next call's `slug`.\n\nThe address stays **app-relative and locale-less**. Routes are locale-prefixed and\na Pages deployment adds a base path, neither of which the server knows; baking\n`/en/` in would send ko users cross-locale and break under `basePath`. That property\nwas already true and undocumented, and is now written into `mcp/README.md`.\n\n**Rejected alternative**: `/topology/?mode=focus&p=…`, the council's literal\nwording. It opens a read-only drawer rather than the meaning editor, which is a\nbehaviour change the council did not argue for and (92).5 did not overturn. Read\nthe council's phrase as \"the `/topology` address that focuses the node\".\n\n**Rubric**: 18/24 — Problem insight 4 · User moment 4 · Differentiation 2 ·\nOntology value 2 · Agent value 4 · Verification 2. No fatal zeros. Ontology value\nis an honest 2: no graph semantics change, but the handoff's update path stops\nlying. Verification is 2 because the installed-app follow of the emitted URL has\nnot been run.\n\n**Gate**: `tests/contract/mcp-emitted-href.contract.test.ts` scans every non-test\nsource in `mcp/src` and `cli/src` for retired routes, taking the list verbatim from\n`.claude/rules/forbidden.md` and decision (91). It excuses whole comment lines and\nnot trailing comments, because explaining what an address replaced is how this\nrepository documents a repair while the code is what ships. It also asserts the\nscanned set is non-empty and still contains the emitting module, and that a\n`/topology/` href is still emitted — so deleting the emission cannot pass as fixing\nit. Probed both ways: reverting line 1522 turns it red, and planting\n`'/settings/profile'` in `cli/src` turns it red.\n\n**Falsifier**: within one dogfood week, an agent-relayed `builder.href` that, opened\nagainst the workbench origin and locale, fails to open the meaning editor on the\nfocused node — deeplink-miss toast, wrong node, or no editor — disproves the address\nchoice on the spot.\n\n**Status**: valid · installed-app follow of the emitted URL pending\n\n---\n\n## 2026-08-24 — The rejected URL scheme gets a gate, not a decision-record trigger\n\n**Prior record**: the 2026-08-24 council rejecting `ontology-atlas://` recorded, in\npassing, that `scripts/check-decision-record.mjs` fires only on added or deleted\n`app/` routes and two `CONTRACT_FILES` — so registering a scheme in\n`src-tauri/tauri.conf.json` would pass `decisions:check` under a green \"no council\ntrigger in this change\". The council left it as a `/gate-probe` candidate.\n\n**Rejected alternative**: adding the Tauri config to that trigger list. It was the\nobvious repair and it is the wrong one — it would demand a decision record for every\nwindow size, icon, and bundle tweak, and a gate that cries on everything is one\npeople learn to silence.\n\n**Decision**: `tests/contract/url-scheme-rejected.contract.test.ts` enforces the\ndecision that was actually made and stays silent about everything else in the file.\nIt checks the deep-link plugin config, raw `CFBundleURLTypes` in both the bundle\nconfig and `Info.plist`, the plugin dependency on the Rust and npm sides, and any\nminted `ontology-atlas://` address in shipped source. Probed three ways — plugin\nblock, `Info.plist` key, Cargo dependency — each turns it red.\n\nThe scheme may legitimately return: the council recorded a falsifier and re-entry\nconditions. The route back is to overturn that record first, per `forbidden.md`\n(\"change the rule itself first. Do not create a silent exception in code\"), and\ndeleting this file is part of the overturn. Deleting a file is a diff a reviewer\nsees; a registration slipped into a config value is not.\n\n**Falsifier**: if this gate ever fires on a change that is not registering a URL\nscheme, its shape is wrong and it should be narrowed rather than exempted.\n\n**Status**: valid\n\n---\n\n## 2026-08-24 — PO pass: hold the ACP events-to-Channel move until one session is measured (6/24)\n\n**Prior decision**: (25) 2026-08-17, \"an exit event closes only the session it was\nborn in\", standing, falsifier unobserved. Its recorded dissent already reasoned\nabout the transport this proposal wants to replace — \"Tauri events are already\nfiltered by session id\" — so per-session filtering is a considered design here,\nnot an oversight. Nothing in that record is overturned.\n\n**Observed phenomenon**: `spawn_acp_line_pump` (`src-tauri/src/lib.rs`) emits one\nTauri event per line of child-process stdout and stderr, at two call sites\n(`acp://message`, `acp://stderr`). `acp::MAX_LINE_BYTES` is 16 MiB, so a single\npayload can in principle be that large, and Tauri event payloads are always JSON\nstrings delivered by evaluating JavaScript. `app.emit` broadcasts, and\n`src/shared/lib/tauri-acp.ts:195` filters with `event.payload.sessionId === sessionId`,\nso with N concurrent sessions each line is delivered N times and discarded N−1\ntimes. The official documentation states plainly that \"the event system is not\ndesigned for low latency or high throughput situations\" and names `Channel` as\nwhat Tauri itself uses for child-process output.\n\n**User problem — none recorded**: no ledger entry, and no observation anywhere in\nthis repository, of agent output arriving slowly, stuttering, arriving out of\norder, or being dropped. `.ontology-atlas/` transcripts are gitignored and absent\nfrom this checkout, so no seat could produce evidence either way. **This proposal\narrived from reading the framework's documentation, not from watching anyone use\nthe product** — which is the exact shape this repository is strict about.\n\n**Phenomenon/problem tests**: difference **FAIL** — remove the phenomenon and no\none is identified losing a decision, understanding, or handoff. Second\nobservation — a real instance would show as stuttering output during a verbose\nsession, a `dropped-line:` notice, or CPU spent in the webview while an agent\ntalks; none observed. Solution independence **FAIL** — the statement names the\nmechanism (`Channel`) rather than a problem that would survive another solution.\n\n**Audience and moment**: someone running a long, verbose ACP agent inside the\ninstalled app, watching its output stream. Real, but no triggering instance has\nbeen observed.\n\n**Current alternative**: the events work. Oversized lines already have a bounded\npath — `read_bounded_line` caps at `MAX_LINE_BYTES` and reports `dropped-line:`\nrather than emitting an unbounded payload — so the 16 MiB ceiling is a bound, not\nan observed event size. ACP protocol lines are JSON-RPC messages, ordinarily\nkilobytes.\n\n**Ontology value**: none. No concept, relation, evidence, provenance, or update\npath becomes clearer. The transport is invisible to the graph.\n\n**Agent value**: none. These events are consumed by this app's own UI. No MCP or\nCLI contract, no agent-facing typed surface, changes at all.\n\n**Simplification**: a channel would remove the broadcast-then-discard filtering on\nthe frontend. Against that, `acp://exit` and `acp://notice` must stay events\nbecause decision (25) built session-ownership semantics on them, so the result is\ntwo transports where there is now one.\n\n**Verification**: nameable but unrun. Instrument one real dogfood session for line\ncount, lines per second, and the size distribution of emitted lines against the\n16 MiB bound.\n\n**Score**: Problem insight 2 · User moment 2 · Differentiation 0 · Ontology value\n0 · Agent value 0 · Verification 2 = **6/24** (fatal zeros: Ontology value, Agent\nvalue)\n\n**Escalation**: mechanically required — below 18 and two fatal zeros — and\ndeliberately **not convened**, citing the standing 2026-07-27 interpretation that\nconvening a council to reach \"do not build\" is process theatre. A build direction\nwould require a fresh pass escalating on its own numbers.\n\n**Verdict**: **Investigate first.** One measurement decides it, and it is cheap:\nduring the next real dogfood ACP session, record how many lines are emitted, at\nwhat rate, and the p50/p99 emitted line size. If p99 is kilobytes and the rate is\nmodest, close this as measured-and-not-a-problem — that record is worth having, so\nthe next person who reads the Tauri documentation does not re-raise it from the\nsame quote. If p99 approaches megabytes, or a session sustains a rate where the\nwebview visibly falls behind, reopen with a fresh pass.\n\n**No-gos if it is ever built**: do not move `acp://exit` or `acp://notice` —\ndecision (25) built session-ownership semantics on them; do not touch the\ngeneration and process-id ownership check; keep `MAX_LINE_BYTES` bounded.\n\n**Recorded dissent**: the framework's own documentation names child-process output\nas the canonical channel case, and waiting for a user to notice a latency problem\nmeans shipping a known mechanism mismatch until it hurts someone. The counter is\nthat this repository has been burned more often by unmeasured churn than by\ndocumented-but-unobserved mismatches, and a 16 MiB bound that nothing has been\nobserved to approach is a ceiling, not a load.\n\n**Falsifier**: one observed session where agent output visibly lags the agent, or\none `dropped-line:` notice traced to payload size rather than a malformed line,\nproves the hold wrong.\n\n**Status**: valid · one measured session pending\n\n---\n\n## 2026-08-24 — `opt-level = \"s\"` measured and rejected: it halves the hashing this product does\n\n**Prior record**: the 2026-08-24 release-profile change adopted `lto`,\n`codegen-units = 1` and `strip`, and left `opt-level = \"s\"` out with the reason\n\"1.5 MB of an 89 MB bundle does not justify trading throughput nobody has\nmeasured\". This is that measurement, and it closes the question rather than\nleaving it open.\n\n**Observed measurement**: a standalone benchmark outside the repository, linking\nthe same `sha2 0.10.9` the app uses and hashing 512 MiB in 1 MiB chunks — the\nshape `inspect_project_source` produces when it walks a vault — built twice with\nidentical settings except the optimisation level:\n\n| Profile | Throughput |\n|---|---|\n| `opt-level = 3` (shipped) | **582 MiB/s** |\n| `opt-level = \"s\"` (candidate) | **277 MiB/s** |\n\n`opt-level = \"s\"` costs **52% of the throughput** on the one CPU-bound path this\nproduct owns.\n\n**Decision**: do not adopt `opt-level = \"s\"`. It buys 1.5 MB — 1.7% of an 89 MB\nbundle whose largest component is a 61 MB sidecar — and pays for it by doubling\nthe time spent hashing the owner's own files.\n\n**Honest scale**: `SOURCE_INVENTORY_MAX_HASH_BYTES` caps one inventory at 32 MiB,\nso in absolute terms the difference per scan is roughly 55ms against 115ms. Small.\nThe reason to refuse is not the milliseconds but the ratio: a 2× regression on the\nproduct's own hot path, bought with 1.7% of a bundle, is a trade that gets worse\nevery time the vault grows.\n\n**Rejected alternative**: adopting it anyway and revisiting if a large vault ever\nfeels slow. Rejected because the number is already known — waiting would mean\nchoosing to be surprised later by something measured today.\n\n**Falsifier**: if a future profile ever makes the Rust half of the binary the\nbundle's dominant component — that is, if the sidecar shrinks below it — the trade\nchanges and this should be re-measured rather than cited.\n\n**Status**: valid · question closed, not deferred\n\n---\n\n## 2026-08-24 — The capability keeps two core sets, and this time it was measured\n\n**Prior record**: the 2026-08-24 runtime-hardening change replaced `core:default`\nwith five core sets and said so plainly: `core:path`, `core:window` and\n`core:webview` were \"retained without an observed frontend caller — dropping them\nis a further narrowing that needs installed-app proof, not a guess\". This is that\nproof, and it closes the item.\n\n**Static evidence**: nothing in `src/` or `app/` imports `@tauri-apps/api/path`,\n`/window`, `/webview`, `/menu`, `/tray` or `/image`. The modules that *are*\nimported invoke only `plugin:event|*` (`core`'s `event` module), `plugin:app|*`\n(`getVersion`), and `plugin:resources|close` — the last from `core.js`, reachable\nonly by constructing a `Resource`, which nothing here does. `Channel` is not a\n`Resource`, so even adopting one later would not need that grant.\n\n**Runtime evidence**: a packaged, ad-hoc-signed build with the three permissions\nremoved launches, loads the dogfood vault, renders WebView content, and logs\n**zero** permission denials.\n\n**The reason it was safe to remove at all**: capabilities gate JS-to-Rust IPC\nonly. This app sizes and positions its window from Rust — `set_size`,\n`set_position`, `maximize` on a `WebviewWindow` — which no capability governs. The\n`core:window` grant was protecting a door the app never walks through.\n\n**Decision**: the main window's capability is `core:event:default`,\n`core:app:default`, `updater:default`, `process:allow-restart`. Four permissions,\neach with a named caller.\n\n**A finding worth carrying**: `@tauri-apps/api/core` calls `plugin:resources|close`\ninternally, and `core:resources` is *not* granted. Nothing constructs a `Resource`\ntoday, so it never fires — but this is exactly the shape that would fail silently\nand late. Anyone introducing a `Resource` subclass must grant it in the same\nchange; the readiness gate's allow-by-name list is where that decision gets made\nvisible.\n\n**Rejected alternative**: leaving the three granted on the theory that unused\npermissions are harmless. Rejected because a capability file is a claim about what\nthe webview may do, and a claim nobody has checked is not a security boundary — it\nis a comment.\n\n**Falsifier**: any permission denial in the app log, or a webview feature that\nsilently stops working, means the narrowing was wrong. The retreat is to grant the\nspecific set with its caller named, not to restore `core:default`.\n\n**Verification**: `check-desktop-readiness.mjs` binds the required set and was\nprobed both ways — removing a needed permission turns it red, and granting an\nunreviewed one turns it red.\n\n**Status**: valid · measured on a packaged build\n\n---\n\n## 2026-08-24 — ACP output measured: events are the right transport, and the question is closed\n\n**Prior decision**: the 2026-08-24 PO pass held the events-to-`Channel` move at\n6/24 with the verdict \"investigate first\", and named the exact measurement that\nwould settle it — line count, rate, and p50/p99 emitted line size during one real\nsession. This is that measurement. The hold is resolved, not extended.\n\n**How it was measured**: a harness outside the repository spawned the same ACP\nadapter the app spawns (`npx @agentclientprotocol/claude-agent-acp@0.70.0`),\ncompleted a real turn against the dogfood vault, and recorded every stdout line's\nbyte length and arrival time — the exact stream `spawn_acp_line_pump` reads.\n\n**First attempt was wrong and is recorded as such**: it sent `session/prompt` with\na hardcoded session id that the adapter never issued, so no turn ran and it\nmeasured a handshake. Reporting 0.6 lines/second from that would have been a\nmeasurement of nothing. The harness was fixed to use the session id the adapter\nactually returns, and the numbers below come from a run where the adapter reported\na `stopReason`.\n\n| | Measured |\n|---|---|\n| Lines after the prompt | 6 over 24.4s |\n| Rate | **0.2–0.3 lines/second** |\n| p50 line size | 988 B |\n| p99 / largest line | **47,274 B (46 KB)** |\n| Largest line against `MAX_LINE_BYTES` | **0.28%** of the 16 MiB ceiling |\n\n**Decision**: keep the events. The documentation's concern is \"low latency or high\nthroughput\", and this stream is three orders of magnitude away from either. p99 is\nkilobytes, not megabytes, and the 46 KB peak is the capabilities response, not\nstreamed content. `Channel` would add a second transport — `acp://exit` and\n`acp://notice` must stay events because decision (25) built session-ownership\nsemantics on them — in exchange for nothing measurable.\n\n**What this record is for**: the proposal came from reading Tauri's documentation,\nand the same page will be read again. The next person finds this number instead of\nraising the same quote.\n\n**Honest limit**: one turn, one short prompt, one adapter. A long agentic session\nwith many tool calls emits more lines. The margin absorbs a great deal — even 100×\nthis rate is 20–30 lines/second, which events carry without trouble — but the\nfalsifier below is what reopens it, not an argument.\n\n**Falsifier**: an observed session where agent output visibly lags the agent, or a\n`dropped-line:` notice traced to payload size rather than a malformed line. Either\nreopens this with a fresh pass.\n\n**Status**: valid · measured and closed\n\n---\n\n## 2026-08-24 — The install progress this repo built to cure \"quiet waiting\" had never once been delivered\n\n**Prior record**: the owner reported \"quiet waiting\" on 2026-08-20 — an install\nthat shows four characters and no progress. The repository answered it properly:\n`acp_install_cli` pumps npm's stderr line by line on a spawned thread rather than\nusing `.output()`, and `ensure_managed_node` reports downloaded bytes every 250ms\nagainst a known total. Both carry long comments arguing why, including \"do not\nfabricate percentages; push the exact lines the tool actually emitted\". Neither\ndecision is overturned here. They were correct, and they never ran.\n\n**Observed phenomenon**: a `#[tauri::command]` without `(async)` executes **inline\non the macOS main thread**. Established from the crate source, not the prose:\n`tauri-macros` expands the blocking wrapper to call the function body directly,\nand `wry` receives `invoke` inside `webView:startURLSchemeTask:`, which WebKit\ndelivers on the main thread.\n\nThe consequence that matters is one step further. Events emitted from *any*\nthread are delivered by the **main thread's event loop**. So while\n`acp_install_node` blocks that thread downloading 52 MB, every progress event its\nown pump emits sits in a queue nobody is draining. The window cannot be moved,\nresized or closed; input is ignored; macOS eventually paints the beachball. When\nthe command finally returns, the whole progress history replays at once.\n\nThe feature was built, argued, merged — and could not work, because the command\nwhose progress it reported was holding the thread that would have delivered it.\n\n**Decision**: thirteen commands move to `#[tauri::command(async)]` — the two\ninstallers, the four ACP doctor commands, runtime detection, project-source\ninspection, the two curl-backed LLM commands, the three network git commands, and\nMCP verification. None of them takes `State<'_, T>`, so the borrowed-argument\nconstraint the documentation warns about does not apply, and the frontend changes\nnothing because `invoke` was already promise-based.\n\n**`pick_vault_directory` deliberately stays synchronous.** `rfd::FileDialog::pick_folder`\nopens an `NSOpenPanel`, which macOS requires on the main thread and which runs its\nown modal event loop — the UI stays responsive *because* that command blocks\nthere. It is the reason this is a named list rather than a blanket rule, and the\nreason the gate asserts the exception as loudly as the rule.\n\n**Also repaired in the same pass**: `open_external_url` spawned `open` and dropped\nthe `Child` without waiting. Dropping a `Child` does not reap it, so every clicked\nexternal link left a zombie in the process table for the rest of the session,\nbounded only by how many links someone followed. It is now reaped on a detached\nthread. The `reveal_in_finder` path in the same file already did this correctly\nwith `.status()`.\n\n**Rejected alternative**: making all 44 commands async. `pick_vault_directory`\nproves a blanket rule wrong, and ~35 commands finish in milliseconds where the\nattribute would buy nothing and cost a thread hop.\n\n**Falsifier**: if a user reports the window freezing during an install, a chat, or\na git pull after this change, the analysis is wrong somewhere and the next step is\nto measure which thread the body is actually on rather than to add more\nattributes.\n\n**Verification**: `tests/contract/command-main-thread.contract.test.ts` names each\ncommand and its reason, and was probed both ways — reverting `git_pull` to the\nbare attribute turns it red, and making `pick_vault_directory` async turns it red.\n`cargo test` 222 pass. The packaged app was rebuilt and launched: window at\n1512x900, vault watcher running, WebView content loaded from a real vault.\n\n**Honest limit**: the freeze was established from the crate source and the\nmechanism, not from a stopwatch on a 52 MB download. What is measured is that the\napp still works with the thirteen commands moved; what is reasoned is that they\nwere freezing it before.\n\n**Status**: valid · progress-during-install not yet watched by a human\n\n## 2026-08-24 — Codex returns to in-app chat, because the checkpoint moved to the server\n\n**Convened because**: decision (111) removed Codex from in-app chat and wrote down\nthe exact price of return. This record pays it, and records what the acceptance run\nfound on the way.\n\n**Prior decision**: (111) stands and is now satisfied, not overturned. Its restore\ncondition was *\"an app-owned MCP proxy or server capability token reliably pauses\nevery Codex Atlas write… proves reject/allow on a disposable vault.\"* (113) built\nthat checkpoint inside the server that performs the write. This record is the\nmeasurement.\n\n**What was measured** — installed build, disposable vault at `~/Downloads/atlas-accept`\n(10 Markdown files), Codex registered by the **vault's own** `.codex/config.toml`,\nwhich is the registration path (111) could not gate:\n\n| answer | wire | vault |\n|---|---|---|\n| reject | `Error: The change was not approved (decline). No change was made` | unchanged, 10 files |\n| allow | `{\"ok\":true,\"slug\":\"wire-probe\",\"changed\":true}` | the file appeared |\n\n**What the run caught that no unit test had**: the gate refused **yes**. The\nelicitation declared `required: ['confirm']`, and an ACP permission card has no form\nto fill — `codex-acp` maps 「allow once」 to `action: 'accept'` with no content, so a\nperson's approval arrived as a decline. Codex reported it verbatim: *\"the permission\nresponse was invalid because it lacked the required `confirm` field.\"* A checkpoint\nthat cannot be passed is a wall, and a wall teaches people to route around the gate\nrather than answer it. `action` is now the answer; the boolean only overrides it.\n\n**Decision (accountable: owner)**: `codex-acp` joins `CHAT_ELIGIBLE`. `ISOLATION` and\n`CHAT_ELIGIBLE` stay separate lists — controlling a config directory is not the same\nclaim as the resulting configuration holding a write, and conflating them is what\n2026-08-24 was about.\n\n**Recorded dissent**: one green acceptance is a smaller sample than the two red\nmeasurements that removed Codex, and the gate depends on a third party continuing to\nforward `mcpServer/elicitation/request`. The counter is that the checkpoint no longer\nlives in that third party's configuration: it lives in the server, fails closed when a\nclient declares no `elicitation` capability, and therefore degrades to refusal rather\nthan to a silent write.\n\n**Falsifier**: one Atlas write from an app-opened Codex session that changes the vault\nwithout a card, or one approval that does not reach the server, returns Codex to the\nstate (111) left it in.\n\n**Revisit**: when `codex-acp` changes how it forwards elicitation, or when a second\nruntime asks to join this list.\n\n**Status**: valid\n\n---\n\n## 2026-08-24 — The first-run card gets a door for people who already have code\n\n**Convened because**: this adds a main affordance to the first surface a stranger\nreads, and the 2026-08-02 first-run-card record put **\"adding or removing an affordance\"** in that\nslice's no-go list. That no-go was an appetite boundary for one day of work, not a\npermanent ban — but overturning it needs a record, which is this one.\n\n**Accountable**: the owner, who asked for it in their own words and, when told the\nprior no-go stood in the way, answered that we should go ahead and do it.\nThat instruction is the grounds; this record is not a self-approval.\n\n**Prior decision**: 2026-08-02 stands in every other respect — the attention\nhierarchy, the demoted gauge, the glossary rows, the census provenance. Only the\nno-go on affordance count is overturned, and only for this one addition. The same\nrecord's own follow-up — *\"point the CLI bridge at my repository\"* — is what this\ndoor finally answers.\n\n**Observed phenomenon** (measured on the shipped card): of its four actions —\nopen my folder, create a new folder, look around here, take the two-minute tour —\n**none makes an ontology from a repository that already exists**. Opening a folder\nwith no Markdown gives an empty map; creating one gives five seeded example nodes.\nThe only real path is the folded \"to start from a terminal\" row, and `cliBridgeSourceOnly`\nhas that row telling app users it excludes them.\n\n**User problem**: someone who **already has a codebase** opens the app and there is\nno route on screen from their code to a map of it. This product's north star is\n\"link → app → within five minutes an agent cites a node in my vault\", and the first\nfive minutes had no starting line.\n\n**Why the app hands this to an agent rather than doing it itself**\n\nThe app never calls MCP tools. It is a static export with a Tauri bridge; MCP is the\nagents' surface. Making the door analyse a repository in TypeScript would create a\nsecond canonical implementation of `analyze_repo_structure`, which `AGENTS.md`\nforbids and which a contract test would then have to hold in step forever.\n\nSo the door does what the product's own thesis says: **the agent does the work\nthrough MCP, and the person approves each write.** That is not a workaround, it is\nthe shape this product argues for. It also makes the owner's original sentence\nliteral — *\"press this and it just proceeds, connecting the agents and so on.\"*\n\n**What it does**: pick the folder (the existing picker, unchanged) → open the agent\nconversation → put the bootstrap instruction in as the person's own first turn.\nThe agent then runs `analyze_repo_structure`, proposes typed nodes, and **every\nwrite asks** through the checkpoint decision (113) built and decision (114) proved.\n\n**What it deliberately does not do**: it does not analyse the repository itself, it\ndoes not write a single node without the person answering a card, and on the web —\nwhere there is no agent to hand to — the door is **absent** rather than present and\ndisabled. A door that cannot open is worse than no door.\n\n**Recorded dissent**: the instruction is sent on the person's behalf by a button\npress, and a prefilled composer they press themselves would be more literal about\nconsent. The counter is that the button's own label states exactly what will be\nasked, the message appears in the transcript as their own turn so nothing is\nhidden, and every write still stops for an answer. A button labelled \"make a map\nfrom my code\" that then makes you press send again is a half-promise, which is the\ndefect class this whole round has been repairing.\n\n**Falsifier**: someone presses the door, watches the agent propose, and refuses the\nwrites — or presses it and cannot tell what was about to happen. Either means the\nhandoff explains itself less well than the folded CLI row it replaced.\n\n**Revisit**: when the app gains a way to call MCP itself, at which point the\nanalysis could move in-process and this record's central reasoning expires.\n\n**Status**: valid\n\n---\n## 2026-08-24 — The map moves inside the project, into a folder named `atlas`\n\n**Context**: the product shipped two contradictory answers to \"where does a map\nlive\". The installed app's \"just start\" created `~/Documents/Ontology Atlas/`,\noutside any project (`src-tauri/src/lib.rs`), while the CLI's `init` and this\nrepository's own vault sit inside the repository. The owner asked directly:\n*\"if the user picks a particular project, should our folder be created at that\nproject's root, rather than somewhere outside it?\"*\n\n**Decision**: when someone points the 「make a map from my code」 door at a\nproject, the vault is created at `<project>/atlas/`. The name is `atlas`, chosen\nby the owner over `docs/ontology`: *\"docs is used so much I think it would just\nget deleted.\"* Nothing is created until the exact path is on screen and the\nperson presses the button beside it. An existing `atlas` folder is continued, not\noverwritten, and the screen says which of the two is happening.\n\n**Why inside**: with the map outside the repository, a colleague who clones the\ncode does not get the meaning, and a change to the code lands in a pull request\nwhile the change to its meaning does not. The map then rots invisibly. That\ndirectly contradicts the product's own claim that Git is the source of truth and\nthat people judge meaning in plain files and diffs. Inside, the two travel\ntogether and are reviewed in one diff.\n\n**Why a visible name, not a dot-folder**: what is hidden does not get read, and\nthis product's whole argument is that a person opens these files.\n\n**Recorded dissent**: a product name at a repository root is close to a one-way\ndoor. If Atlas is ever renamed, that folder is already sitting in other people's\nrepositories, and no later decision can reach them. The counter is that a map\nswept away in a routine `docs/` tidy-up is a worse failure than an awkward name,\nand the owner judged the deletion risk to be the real one.\n\n**Falsifier**: someone deletes or gitignores the `atlas` folder because they read\nit as vendor litter, or a team refuses the folder at their repository root. Either\nmeans the name bought recognisability at too high a price.\n\n**Not settled here**: the app's own \"just start\" path still writes to\n`~/Documents/Ontology Atlas/`. That surface has no project to sit inside, so this\nrecord does not move it; the inconsistency stands and is named for a later pass.\n\n**Status**: valid\n\n---\n\n## 2026-08-24 — The door follows \"hasn't built one yet\", not \"has never opened a folder\"\n\n**Context**: the 「make a map from my code」 door shipped inside the first-run\ncard, which is gated on `recentVaults.length === 0` — this computer has never\nopened a folder. The owner rejected that audience: *\"shouldn't it be person B who\nhas opened folders many times and still hasn't made one?\"*\n\n**Decision**: the card keeps its rule, and the door gets its own. It appears\nwherever a vault is open and nothing in it points at real code, however many times\nthat person has opened folders, and disappears once a map with connected code\nexists.\n\n**Why the two conditions separate**: \"never connected\" is right for *browsing\nguidance*. The sample switch and the tour teach someone who has not looked around\nyet, and pushing them at a returning person is noise. It is wrong for *unfinished\nwork*. Somebody who opened a folder, saw an empty map and gave up has opened\nfolders more than a first-timer: repeated opening is evidence of struggling, not\nof being finished. The old rule hid this door from precisely the person it was\nbuilt for.\n\n**Recorded dissent**: a second home for one action risks two things claiming to be\nthe same door and drifting apart. The counter is that both render one component\nwith one label and one flow, and the variant only changes its size so that it does\nnot out-shout the person's own tree.\n\n**Falsifier**: the row is measured as ignored by people with an unbuilt map, or it\nis reported as clutter by someone who has deliberately chosen not to connect code.\nThe second would mean \"no source bound\" is too broad a reading of \"unfinished\".\n\n**Status**: valid\n\n---\n## 2026-08-24 — Picking a project opens the map inside it, and says so\n\n**Context**: the owner asked, on reading the previous record, *\"so now, whether\ninside the project or outside it, if there is an atlas folder we read it and draw\nthe ontology?\"* The answer at that moment was no. `PROJECT_VAULT_DIR` was read\nonly by the door's own flow; \"open a folder\" did no subfolder detection at all and\ntreated whatever was picked as the vault.\n\n**The hole this closes was opened by the decision above it.** While maps lived\noutside every project there was exactly one folder worth picking. That is wrong: pointing\nthe vault at a project root found the concepts inside its `atlas/` folder, because\nthe reader walks subdirectories. The real damage is quieter. Every other `.md`\ncarrying frontmatter anywhere in the repository joins the map, and the vault root\nbecomes the project root, so later writes and the `.ontology-atlas/` records land\nbeside the source instead of inside the map folder. The notice costs one line and keeps the\nsubstitution inspectable.\n\n**Recorded dissent**: an explicit prompt (\"this project has a map folder, open\nit?\") would be more literal about consent than a notice after the fact. The\ncounter is that this is a read, not a write, it lands the person on the map they\nalready made, and a confirmation on every open of a project is a toll charged\nforever to prevent a mistake the notice already exposes. If the notice proves\ninsufficient the prompt is the next step, not a redesign.\n\n**Falsifier**: somebody reports being surprised by which folder opened, or\ndeliberately wants the project root read as a vault and cannot get it. The second\nwould mean the redirect needs an escape hatch.\n\n**Status**: valid\n\n---\n## 2026-08-25 — `init` may only wire the project it was actually run inside\n\n**Observed damage**: running `node cli/src/index.mjs init <somewhere-else>` from\nthis repository rewrote **this repository's** `.mcp.json` and\n`.codex/config.toml` to point at the scratch vault. Nothing asked, nothing\nwarned, and the edit was noticed only because the files were under version\ncontrol. Anyone whose project is not in Git would have lost their agent\nconfiguration without a trace.\n\n**Cause**: the cwd write was guarded by `cwdPath !== canonicalTarget` — a\ncondition true of every unrelated directory on the disk.\n\n**Decision**: `init` writes agent config into the current directory only when the\nvault is created **inside** it. When the vault lands outside, cwd is left\nuntouched and the command says so and why. The vault's own config is always\nwritten, unchanged from before.\n\n**Why containment rather than difference**: the cwd write exists for one real\nflow — *\"I am standing in my project, put a vault inside it\"* — where cwd is the\ncodebase the vault describes. When the vault is elsewhere, cwd is merely where\nthe person happened to stand, and repointing its agents at an unrelated vault is\na silent edit to a project this command was never asked to touch.\n\n**Also corrected**: the closing summary promised that \"both your codebase root\n(cwd) and the vault folder\" were wired. That became false the moment cwd was\ndeliberately skipped, so it now names what was actually written.\n\n**Recorded dissent**: someone running `init ../other-project/vault` from their\ncodebase may genuinely want their current project bound to a vault kept beside\nit, and now has to run the command from somewhere else or wire it by hand. The\ncounter is that this reading cannot be distinguished from the accidental one, and\nbetween silently editing an uninvolved project and asking someone to re-run a\ncommand, the recoverable failure is the right one to choose.\n\n**Falsifier**: a report of keeping the vault deliberately outside the codebase and\nfinding the extra step onerous.
